| S4.226 | **List item — primitive.** Every other R is the primitive token of R (S2.124, WS-trimmed, string-literal errors surface). | `items[3]:⏎␠␠- 1⏎␠␠- a: 1⏎␠␠- text` → `[1.0,{"a":1.0},"text"]`; probes: `␠␠-␠␠␠x␠␠` → `"x"`; `␠␠- - x` → `"- x"`; `␠␠- "a: b"` → `"a: b"`; `␠␠- a"b: c` → `"a\"b: c"`; `␠␠- "a:b" x` → `ERR: Unexpected characters after closing quote` | `src/decode/decoders.rs:473` | fx_dec_arrays_nested_02, fx_dec_arrays_nested_16, fx_dec_arrays_nested_20, toonerr_blank_in_list, decnum_inline_and_tabular toonedge_item_quote_hides_colon |
| S4.227 | **Event sequences of the item shapes** (t = item depth). | `-` → `SO EO`; `- ` → `SO EO`; `- x` → `P("x")`; `- [2]: a,b` → `SA(2) P(a) P(b) EA`; `- id: 1` + sibling `name: Ada` → `SO K(id) P(1.0) K(name) P("Ada") EO`; `- c:` + child `d: 3` at t+2 → `SO K(c) SO K(d) P(3.0) EO EO`; `- users[2]{id,name}:` + two rows at t+2 + sibling `status: active` → `SO K(users) SA(2) SO K(id) P(1.0) K(name) P("Ada") EO SO K(id) P(2.0) K(name) P("Bob") EO EA K(status) P("active") EO`; `- "k"[1]{"x"}:` → the same shape with `K(k,q)` and `K(x,q)` | `src/decode/decoders.rs:369`, `src/decode/decoders.rs:479` | toonerr_list_dash_only, toonerr_list_obj_fields, toonerr_list_obj_first_tabular, fx_dec_arrays_nested_05, fx_dec_arrays_nested_09 |
| S4.240 | **Path expansion — when and where.** Only with `--expand-paths safe`. It runs once over the finished value tree (S3.25), after the whole document decoded without error: a primitive is unchanged; an array has each item expanded in order; an object is rebuilt entry by entry (S4.242). It applies at every level: root objects, nested objects, objects inside arrays (tabular rows, list items), so tabular field names, list-item keys and array-header keys all take part. A root primitive or root array of primitives passes through unchanged. | `a.b.c: 1` → `{"a":{"b":{"c":1.0}}}`; `data.meta.items[2]: a,b` → `{"data":{"meta":{"items":["a","b"]}}}`; `rows[2]{a.b,c}:⏎␠␠1,2⏎␠␠3,4⏎l[1]:⏎␠␠- p.q: 1` → `{"rows":[{"a":{"b":1.0},"c":2.0},{"a":{"b":3.0},"c":4.0}],"l":[{"p":{"q":1.0}}]}`; `user.name: Ada` with `--expand-paths off` → `{"user.name":"Ada"}`; probes: `hello` → `"hello"`; `[1]:⏎␠␠- [1]:⏎␠␠␠␠- a.b: 1` → `[[{"a":{"b":1.0}}]]` | `src/decode/expand.rs:25`, `src/cli/conversion.rs:59` | fx_dec_path_expansion_01, fx_dec_path_expansion_02, fx_dec_path_expansion_03, fx_dec_path_expansion_04, jsonout_expand_in_arrays, happy_config_expanded, decnum_expand_path, jsonout_expand_indent_2 |
| S4.241 | **Which keys expand.** An entry's key is a path when all three hold: it contains a `.`; the key string is not in the object's quoted-key set (S3.26); every piece between the dots is an identifier segment: first character an ASCII letter or `_`, the rest ASCII letters, digits or `_`, at least one character. Any other key is inserted literally, unchanged. | `full-name.x: 1` → `{"full-name.x":1.0}`; `a.b: 1⏎"c.d": 2` → `{"a":{"b":1.0},"c.d":2.0}`; probes: `a.: 1`, `.a: 1`, `a..b: 1`, `a.1: 1`, `1a.b: 1`, `a.é: 1`, `a.b c: 1`, `a .b: 1`, `.: 1` all stay literal; `A.B_9.c: 1` → `{"A":{"B_9":{"c":1.0}}}`; `a._b: 1` → `{"a":{"_b":1.0}}`; `[1]{"a.b",c.d}:⏎␠␠1,2` → `[{"a.b":1.0,"c":{"d":2.0}}]` | `src/decode/expand.rs:58`, `src/shared/validation.rs:26` | fx_dec_path_expansion_10, fx_dec_path_expansion_11, jsonout_expand_merge |
| S4.242 | **Order inside one object.** Entries are taken in document order. For each entry, FIRST its value is expanded recursively (so an error inside the value is reported before anything about this key), THEN the key is inserted into the new object: a path by S4.243, any other key by S4.244. The new object starts empty. | `a.b.c: 1⏎a.b.d: 2⏎a.e: 3` → `{"a":{"b":{"c":1.0,"d":2.0},"e":3.0}}`; probe: `a: 1⏎a:⏎␠␠x.y: 1⏎␠␠x: 2` → `ERR: Path expansion conflict at key "x": cannot merge object with primitive` (the inner conflict, not the one at `a`); probe: `z: 0⏎a.b: 1⏎m: 5⏎a.c: 2` → `{"z":0.0,"a":{"b":1.0,"c":2.0},"m":5.0}` | `src/decode/expand.rs:46` | fx_dec_path_expansion_05, jsonout_expand_merge toonerr_expand_value_error_first |
| S4.243 | **Path insertion** of segments s1 … sn (n ≥ 2) with value v into an entry list. For s1: if an entry with that key exists and its value is an object, continue inside that object with s2 … sn; if it exists and is not an object: strict → S9.117 naming s1 and the type of the existing value; lenient → the existing value is replaced, at its position, by an empty object and the walk continues inside it; if no entry exists, a new entry (s1, empty object) is appended and the walk continues inside it. The last segment sn is inserted with v by S4.244. | `a: 1⏎a.b: 2` strict → `ERR: Path expansion conflict at segment "a": expected object but found primitive`, lenient → `{"a":{"b":2.0}}`; probes: `a[1]: x⏎a.b: 2` → `…at segment "a": expected object but found array`; `a.b: 1⏎a.b.c: 2` → `…at segment "b": expected object but found primitive`, lenient → `{"a":{"b":{"c":2.0}}}`; `a:⏎␠␠b: 1⏎a.c: 2` → `{"a":{"b":1.0,"c":2.0}}`; `a.b.c:` → `{"a":{"b":{"c":{}}}}` | `src/decode/expand.rs:77` | fx_dec_path_expansion_01, fx_dec_path_expansion_09, fx_dec_path_expansion_12, jsonout_expand_conflict_obj_prim, jsonout_expand_conflict_obj_prim_lenient, toonedge_expand_hash_collision_conflict |
| S4.244 | **Literal insertion** of (k, v) into an entry list. No entry with key k → append (k, v). An entry exists: if the existing value and v are both objects → deep merge (S4.245) into the existing one, which keeps its position; otherwise strict → S9.116 naming k, the type of the EXISTING value, then the type of v; lenient → the existing entry's value is replaced by v, keeping the entry's position (last write wins, first position). This rule also handles plain duplicate keys without any dot. | `a.b: 1⏎a: 2` strict → `ERR: Path expansion conflict at key "a": cannot merge object with primitive`, lenient → `{"a":2.0}`; `a.b: 1⏎a[2]: 2,3` → `…cannot merge object with array`; `a: 1⏎a: 2⏎b: 3` strict → `…at key "a": cannot merge primitive with primitive`, lenient → `{"a":2.0,"b":3.0}`; probes: `a.b: 1⏎a.b: 2` → `…at key "b": cannot merge primitive with primitive`; `a: 1⏎a:` → `…cannot merge primitive with object`; `a[0]:⏎a[0]:` → `…cannot merge array with array`; `a:⏎a:` → `{"a":{}}`; `[1]{a.b,a}:⏎␠␠1,2` → `…at key "a": cannot merge object with primitive`, lenient → `[{"a":2.0}]` | `src/decode/expand.rs:131` | fx_dec_path_expansion_06, fx_dec_path_expansion_07, fx_dec_path_expansion_08, jsonout_expand_conflict_strict, jsonout_expand_conflict_lenient, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand_lenient |
| S4.245 | **Deep merge** of a source object into a target object: the source entries are taken in order and each is inserted into the target's entry list by the rule of S4.244 (object + object merges recursively; anything else conflicts in strict mode or overwrites in place in lenient mode; new keys are appended at the end of the target). Merging happens in both modes. | `a.b: 1⏎a.c: 2⏎a:⏎␠␠d: 3` → `{"a":{"b":1.0,"c":2.0,"d":3.0}}`; probes: `a:⏎␠␠b: 1⏎a:⏎␠␠c: 2` → `{"a":{"b":1.0,"c":2.0}}`; `a:⏎␠␠b: 1⏎a:⏎␠␠b: 2` → `ERR: …at key "b": cannot merge primitive with primitive`, lenient → `{"a":{"b":2.0}}`; lenient `a.b: 1⏎m: 5⏎a:⏎␠␠c: 2⏎␠␠b: 9` → `{"a":{"b":9.0,"c":2.0},"m":5.0}`; `x.y:⏎␠␠p.q: 1⏎x:⏎␠␠y:⏎␠␠␠␠p:⏎␠␠␠␠␠␠r: 2` → `{"x":{"y":{"p":{"q":1.0,"r":2.0}}}}` | `src/decode/expand.rs:164` | jsonout_expand_merge, fx_dec_path_expansion_05, toonerr_expand_repeated_hash_collision |
| S4.246 | **Type names** used in the two conflict messages: `primitive` (string, number, boolean, null), `array`, `object`. | see S4.243, S4.244 | `src/decode/expand.rs:215` | fx_dec_path_expansion_06, fx_dec_path_expansion_07, jsonout_expand_conflict_obj_prim, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand, toonedge_expand_hash_collision_lenient |
| S4.247 | **Duplicate keys, the two paths.** Without expansion, duplicate keys of one object are all written, in document order (the streaming writer does no bookkeeping). With expansion they meet S4.244: strict → conflict (or merge when both are objects), lenient → the last value at the first position. | `a: 1⏎a: 2⏎b: 3`: no flag → an object text with `"a"` twice (`{"a":1.0,"a":2.0,"b":3.0}` in compact form); `--expand-paths safe` → `ERR: Path expansion conflict at key "a": cannot merge primitive with primitive`; plus `--no-strict` → `{"a":2.0,"b":3.0}`; probe: `items[1]{a,a}:⏎␠␠1,2` → `[{"a":1.0,"a":2.0}]` without the flag, the same conflict with it | `src/decode/event_builder.rs:186`, `src/decode/expand.rs:141` | jsonout_duplicate_keys, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand_lenient |
| S4.248 | **Depth cap 256.** Expansion carries a counter c and fails with S9.118 as soon as a step would run with c ≥ 256. The counter: the root value has c = 0; an item of an array with counter c has c+1; an object with counter c is rebuilt at c+1 and each of its entry values has c+2 (so a value nested inside k objects has c = 2k); inserting a path of n segments into an object with counter c runs its walk at c+2, c+3, …, c+n+1 and its final literal insertion at c+n+2; a literal (non-path) insertion into that object runs at c+2; a deep merge started by an insertion at counter m runs at m+1, and each nested merge one higher. The check applies to primitives too. Consequences (all probed, strict and lenient alike): a root-level dotted key is accepted up to 253 segments and rejected from 254; inside an object nested k levels below the root the limit is 253 − 2k segments (k = 1: 251 accepted, 252 rejected; k = 9: 235 / 236); a chain of nested objects whose deepest member holds a primitive is accepted up to 127 objects (root included) and rejected at 128 (when the deepest member is an empty object, 128 are accepted and 129 rejected); a chain of nested list arrays whose innermost member holds a primitive is accepted up to 255 arrays and rejected at 256; two 252-segment root keys whose object values share a key (`…: ⏎␠␠x:⏎␠␠␠␠p: 1` twice) are rejected while 251 segments pass; two 253-segment keys with flat object values are rejected while 252 pass; a mixed chain obeys the same counter: 100 nested objects (the value under the deepest key has c = 200) holding 55 nested list arrays around a primitive pass, 56 are rejected. Without `--expand-paths safe` none of this applies (S2.150). | a 300-segment key `k.k.….k: 1` → `ERR: Path expansion exceeded maximum depth of 256` | `src/decode/expand.rs:11`, `src/decode/expand.rs:29`, `src/decode/expand.rs:84`, `src/decode/expand.rs:138`, `src/decode/expand.rs:170` | jsonout_expand_deep_limit toonedge_expand_segments_253, toonerr_expand_segments_254, toonedge_expand_nested_127, toonerr_expand_nested_128, toonedge_expand_arrays_255, toonerr_expand_arrays_256, toonedge_expand_empty_leaf_128, toonerr_expand_empty_leaf_129, toonedge_expand_mixed_100_objects_55_arrays, toonerr_expand_mixed_100_objects_56_arrays, toonedge_expand_nested_key_251, toonerr_expand_nested_key_252, toonedge_expand_header_key_253, toonerr_expand_header_key_254, toonedge_expand_root_tabular_field_252, toonerr_expand_root_tabular_field_253, toonedge_expand_keyed_tabular_field_250, toonerr_expand_keyed_tabular_field_251, toonedge_expand_merge_flat_252, toonerr_expand_merge_flat_253, toonedge_expand_merge_nested_251, toonerr_expand_merge_nested_252, toonedge_expand_merge3_250, toonerr_expand_merge3_251 |
| S4.249 | **Strict flag in expansion.** The same `--no-strict` that relaxes the scanner and the count checks selects last-write-wins in S4.243 / S4.244. Decode errors always come before expansion errors, because expansion starts only after a complete, error-free decode. | probe: `a.b: 1⏎a: 2⏎l[2]: x` with `--expand-paths safe` → `ERR: Expected 2 inline array items, but got 1`; probe: `a.b: 1⏎a: 2⏎␠␠␠z: 1` → the line-3 indentation message | `src/cli/conversion.rs:63`, `src/decode/expand.rs:99` | fx_dec_path_expansion_08, fx_dec_path_expansion_09, jsonout_expand_conflict_lenient, jsonout_expand_indent_0, jsonout_expand_indent_1, jsonout_expand_indent_4 toonerr_decode_beats_expand |


## S5. Outputs

### S5, part A (command line, effects, I/O errors, --stats): Outputs — framing, streams, success and statistics lines (S5.100–S5.129)

| S5.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5.100 | Encode, no `-o`: stdout receives the TOON lines separated by single LF bytes, followed by exactly one final LF. With zero TOON lines (input `{}`) stdout is the single byte LF. The bytes are identical with and without `--stats` (two different write paths, same result). No BOM, no CR, UTF-8. | mod.rs:50-51, 139-154, 156-191 | happy_readme_users, happy_config_folded, determinism_a, determinism_a_again, stats_small, stats_empty_object, flag_short_combined_e |
| S5.101 | Decode, no `-o`: stdout receives the JSON text pieces concatenated with nothing between them, followed by exactly one final LF (the JSON text itself, pretty or compact per S1.151, has no final LF of its own). Empty input decodes to `{}` + LF. | mod.rs:193-220 | happy_readme_users_decode, happy_config_expanded, flag_short_d, stats_on_decode_ignored, auto_empty_toon_file |
| S5.102 | With `-o FILE` the same bytes as S5.100/S5.101 (including the final LF) go to FILE and stdout receives nothing. When FILE is `/dev/stdout` the bytes appear on stdout through the file path; when it is `/dev/stderr` they appear on stderr before the success line [run]; `/dev/null` discards them. | mod.rs:139-147, 157-174, 194-206 | flag_output_dev_stdout_encode, flag_output_dev_stdout_decode, flag_output_attached, flag_output_equals, flag_output_file_label, stats_with_output, flag_output_dev_null, flag_output_dev_stderr |
| S5.103 | FILE is created when absent and truncated to empty when present (then written from offset 0), but only after the conversion has succeeded: a JSON or TOON error with `-o` leaves an existing FILE untouched and does not create a missing one [run]. Since the whole input is read before FILE is opened, FILE may be the same path as INPUT (in-place conversion works) [run]. No temporary file, no rename, no fsync, no permission change; creation mode is the platform default (0666 masked by umask). | mod.rs:141, 158, 195; mod.rs:46, 100 | flag_output_dev_stdout_encode, usage_output_bad_dir, (case to add: none expressible — the corpus cannot inspect created files; oracle runs in the report) |
| S5.104 | On success without `-o` and without `--stats`, stderr is empty. | mod.rs:75-80, 105-110 | happy_readme_users, happy_readme_users_decode, auto_json_ext, auto_toon_ext, determinism_a |
| S5.105 | Success line, encode with `-o`: after the file is completely written, stderr receives ``Encoded `<in>` → `<out>`\n``: the word `Encoded`, a space, a backtick, `<in>`, a backtick, a space, U+2192, a space, a backtick, `<out>`, a backtick, LF. Worked example (34 bytes): ``Encoded `stdin` → `/dev/stdout`\n``. | mod.rs:76-80 | flag_output_dev_stdout_encode, flag_output_attached, flag_output_equals, flag_output_file_label, stats_with_output |
| S5.106 | Success line, decode with `-o`: the same with the word `Decoded`. Worked example (34 bytes): ``Decoded `stdin` → `/dev/stdout`\n``. | mod.rs:106-110 | flag_output_dev_stdout_decode, flag_cluster_do |
| S5.107 | Labels: `<in>` is the literal `stdin` when INPUT is absent or `-`, otherwise the INPUT word exactly as typed (relative stays relative, `./x` keeps `./`, no quoting or escaping; invalid UTF-8 bytes shown as U+FFFD). `<out>` is the `-o` word exactly as typed, same rules. | mod.rs:222-230, 78, 108 | flag_output_file_label, flag_output_dev_stdout_encode, flag_output_with_dash_input |
| S5.108 | Statistics lines (encode with `--stats`), on stderr, in this order: an empty line (`\n`); `Token estimates: ~<J> (JSON) → ~<T> (TOON)\n`; and, only when D > 0, `Saved ~<D> tokens (-<p1>%)\n`, with J, T, D in plain decimal (no separators, no padding) and `<p1>` per S4.316. The word `tokens` is never singular (`Saved ~1 tokens`). Worked example (68 bytes): `\nToken estimates: ~10 (JSON) → ~6 (TOON)\nSaved ~4 tokens (-40.0%)\n`; with D = 0 (42 bytes): `\nToken estimates: ~1 (JSON) → ~1 (TOON)\n`. | mod.rs:65-69 | stats_small, stats_users, stats_no_savings, stats_empty_object, stats_unicode_whitespace, stats_ratio_1, stats_ratio_2, stats_ratio_3, stats_ratio_4, stats_ratio_5 |
| S5.109 | With `--stats` and `-o` together the stderr order is: empty line, estimates line, optional Saved line, then the success line S5.105. Worked example (102 bytes): ``\nToken estimates: ~10 (JSON) → ~6 (TOON)\nSaved ~4 tokens (-40.0%)\nEncoded `stdin` → `/dev/stdout`\n``. With a regular FILE stdout stays empty and FILE holds the TOON text + LF [run]. | mod.rs:49-80 | stats_with_output, stats_output_dev_null |
| S5.110 | Ordering across the two streams: the complete TOON output (through its final LF) is written and flushed to stdout before the first statistics byte reaches stderr; with both streams on one pipe the text is TOON lines, empty line, estimates, Saved [run]. The goldens capture the streams separately and do not depend on this. | mod.rs:51, 65; mod.rs:148-151 | stats_small, stats_users |
| S5.111 | Runtime failure framing (all of S9.1–S9.19): stderr receives the message followed by one LF; stdout is empty for every failure that happens before writing starts (input, UTF-8, JSON, TOON, output creation); exit code 1. No `error:` prefix, no usage text, no success or statistics lines. | main.rs:3-5 | usage_none, usage_missing_file, usage_output_bad_dir, auto_toon_content_json_ext_fails |
| S5.112 | Stream map: conversion result → stdout (or FILE); help and version → stdout; argv errors, runtime errors, success line, statistics → stderr. Nothing else is ever printed (no warnings about ignored options, no progress, no timing). | mod.rs:65-79, 148-151; clap:error/mod.rs:222-227 | usage_help, usage_version, usage_unknown_flag, stats_small, flag_output_dev_stdout_encode, flag_no_strict_on_encode_ignored |
| S5.113 | Determinism: identical argv and input bytes give identical stdout, stderr and exit code on every run; no timestamp, host name, random value or path other than the typed ones appears in any output. | mod.rs:19-27 | determinism_a, determinism_a_again |

### S5, part B (JSON text input and the two JSON writers): Outputs — JSON text, both writers (S5.50–S5.99)

Names used below: **writer A** is the streaming writer (`src/cli/json_stream.rs`, fed by the decoder's event sequence; used by plain `--decode`);
**writer B** is the tree writer (`src/cli/json_stringify.rs`, fed by the value tree after path expansion; used when `--expand-paths safe` is given).
`N` is the `--indent` value (default 2). `d` is the nesting depth of a container: 0 for the root value, +1 per enclosing container.

| S5.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5.50 | **Writer selection.** Decode mode prints exactly one JSON text. With `--expand-paths off` (the default) it comes from writer A; with `--expand-paths safe` from writer B, whether or not any key contains a dot. `--no-strict` never changes the writer. Both writers receive the same `N`. | `src/cli/conversion.rs:32-42`, `src/cli/mod.rs:85-100` | jsonout_indent_2, jsonout_expand_indent_2, decstr_control_out, decstr_control_out_expand |
| S5.51 | **Framing.** The destination (stdout, or the `-o` file) receives the JSON text followed by exactly ONE LF and nothing else. The JSON text itself never begins or ends with whitespace, so the output ends with exactly one LF at every `N`, also for a root primitive and for `{}`. | `src/cli/mod.rs:193-220` | jsonout_root_array, jsonout_root_empty_array, decstr_root_string, decstr_empty_doc |
| S5.52 | **All or nothing.** The complete JSON text is built in memory before the first byte is written. When scanning, decoding, strict validation or path expansion fails, stdout is EMPTY, stderr carries that failure's one-line message (texts owned by the TOON-decode and path-expansion extractors), exit code 1, and with `-o FILE` the file is not created. Goldens that pin this, stderr verbatim: `Path expansion conflict at key "a": cannot merge object with primitive` (jsonout_expand_conflict_strict); `Path expansion conflict at segment "a": expected object but found primitive` (jsonout_expand_conflict_obj_prim); `Path expansion conflict at key "a": cannot merge primitive with primitive` (jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand); `Path expansion exceeded maximum depth of 256` (jsonout_expand_deep_limit). | `src/cli/conversion.rs:36-41`, `src/cli/mod.rs:100-103` | jsonout_expand_conflict_strict, jsonout_expand_conflict_obj_prim, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand, jsonout_expand_deep_limit |
| S5.53 | **`N` is shared with the TOON reader.** The one `--indent N` is both the JSON output indent and the indentation unit the TOON reader expects of its INPUT. A document indented by 2 therefore cannot be printed with another `N`; the six goldens that try it fail before any JSON is written (S5.52): `N=0` → `Validation error at line 2: Indentation not allowed when indent size is 0, but found 2 spaces` (jsonout_indent_0, jsonout_expand_indent_0); `N=1` → `Expected 2 tabular rows, but got 0` (jsonout_indent_1, jsonout_expand_indent_1); `N=4` → `Validation error at line 2: Indentation must be exact multiple of 4, but found 2 spaces` (jsonout_indent_4, jsonout_expand_indent_4); and `[2]:⏎- a: 1⏎- b: 2` with `N=0` → `Expected 2 list array items, but got 0` (jsonout_root_array_compact). To see output at indent `N` the TOON input itself has to be indented by `N`. | `src/cli/conversion.rs:40-56`, `src/decode/scanner.rs:133-137` | jsonout_indent_0, jsonout_expand_indent_0, jsonout_indent_1, jsonout_expand_indent_1, jsonout_indent_4, jsonout_expand_indent_4, jsonout_root_array_compact |
| S5.54 | **Primitives** (identical in both writers): null → `null`; booleans → `true` / `false`; number → X-C's JSON number text (always a float spelling: `1.0`, `2.5`, `1e+21`, `1e-7`; never a bare integer); string → quoted and escaped per S5.63 (writer A) or S5.64 (writer B). A non-finite number would be written `null` by both writers, but none can arrive from the CLI: TOON tokens such as `1e999`, `inf`, `nan`, `Infinity` decode as STRINGS (`"1e999"`). | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:210-220`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:122-138` | decstr_root_null, decstr_root_true, decnum_root, decnum_extremes, jsonout_root_array |
| S5.55 | **Root primitive.** When the decoded document is a single primitive the JSON text is that primitive alone (then the framing LF), at every `N`: `hello world` → `"hello world"`, `true` → `true`, `null` → `null`, `1e6` → `1000000.0`. Same bytes from writer B. | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:163-186`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:42-46` | decstr_root_string, decstr_root_quoted, decstr_root_true, decstr_root_null, decnum_root jsonout_root_string_expand, jsonout_root_number_expand |
| S5.56 | **Empty containers** are always the two bytes `{}` or `[]`, with no whitespace or newline inside, at EVERY position (root, member value, array element) and at every `N`, in both writers. An empty TOON document decodes to `{}`. | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:56-71`, `src/cli/json_stream.rs:108-123`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:52-56`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:88-91` | decstr_empty_doc, jsonout_root_empty_array, jsonout_indent_2, jsonout_expand_indent_2, toonedge_indent0_indented_lenient jsonout_empty_in_array, jsonout_empty_in_array_expand, jsonout_root_empty_doc_expand, jsonout_root_empty_array_expand |
| S5.57 | **Compact form, `N = 0`.** No whitespace at all is produced: a non-empty array is `[` + its elements joined by `,` + `]`; a non-empty object is `{` + its members joined by `,` + `}`, a member being the escaped key, `:` (no space), the value. Example (both writers, same bytes): `a: 1⏎b[2]: x,y⏎c: "q\"z"⏎d:⏎e[0]:⏎f: null⏎g: true⏎h: false` → `{"a":1.0,"b":["x","y"],"c":"q\"z","d":{},"e":[],"f":null,"g":true,"h":false}`; `[3]: 1,two,true` → `[1.0,"two",true]`; writer B with `a.b: 1⏎a.c[2]: 1,2⏎a.d.e: x` → `{"a":{"b":1.0,"c":[1.0,2.0],"d":{"e":"x"}}}`. | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:36-43`, `src/cli/json_stream.rs:149-158`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:71-78`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:109-118` | toonedge_indent0_flat, decnum_compact, decfmt_exp_compact_and_expand, toonedge_indent0_indented_lenient jsonout_compact_shapes, jsonout_compact_shapes_expand, jsonout_compact_nested_expand, jsonout_root_array_compact_ok, jsonout_root_array_compact_expand |
| S5.58 | **Pretty form, `N > 0`, arrays.** A non-empty array at depth `d` is: `[`; then each element on its own line, written as LF + `(d+1)·N` spaces + the element's text (its depth is `d+1`); a `,` immediately after every element except the last (so the comma ends the line); then LF + `d·N` spaces + `]`. Root example `[3]: 1,two,true` at `N=2`: `[⏎  1.0,⏎  "two",⏎  true⏎]`. | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:88-139`, `src/cli/json_stream.rs:163-197`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:58-70` | jsonout_root_array, jsonout_indent_2, jsonout_expand_indent_2, jsonout_expand_in_arrays |
| S5.59 | **Pretty form, `N > 0`, objects.** A non-empty object at depth `d` is: `{`; then each member on its own line, written as LF + `(d+1)·N` spaces + escaped key + `: ` (colon, ONE space) + the value's text (its depth is `d+1`); a `,` immediately after every member except the last; then LF + `d·N` spaces + `}`. There is never a space before the colon, never a space after a comma, never trailing whitespace, never a blank line. | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:140-162`, `src/cli/json_stream.rs:56-71`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:93-108` | jsonout_indent_2, jsonout_expand_indent_2, jsonout_expand_merge, happy_readme_users_decode, happy_config_expanded, decstr_keys |
| S5.60 | **Where containers open and close.** A container that is a member VALUE opens on the key's line (`"o": {`, `"l": [`); a container that is an array ELEMENT opens on its own line at the element indentation; every non-empty container closes on its own line at its own indentation `d·N`. jsonout_indent_2 shows all of it: `"m": [⏎      1.0,⏎      {⏎        "k": "v",⏎        "z": []⏎      },⏎      [⏎        "a",⏎        "b"⏎      ]⏎    ]`. | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:33-47`, `src/cli/json_stream.rs:88-102`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:61-70` | jsonout_indent_2, jsonout_expand_indent_2, jsonout_expand_in_arrays |

Oracle runs: `N=1` `a:⏎ b:⏎  c: 1` → `{⏎ "a": {⏎  "b": {⏎   "c": 1.0⏎  }⏎ }⏎}`; `N=4` → 4/8/12 spaces; `N=16` `o:⏎` + 16 spaces + `a: 1` → `{⏎` + 16 spaces + `"o": {⏎` + 32 spaces + `"a": 1.0⏎` + 16 spaces + `}⏎}`; `N=3` `[2]:⏎   - [1]:⏎      - [0]:⏎   - [1]: 5` → `[⏎   [⏎      []⏎   ],⏎   [⏎      5.0⏎   ]⏎]` (same bytes from writer B). | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:42`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:140-146` | toonedge_indent1, toonedge_indent4 jsonout_indent_1_ok, jsonout_expand_indent_1_ok, jsonout_indent_4_ok, jsonout_expand_indent_4_ok |
| S5.62 | **Member and element order** in the output is the order in which the decoder produced them; neither writer sorts, groups or deduplicates on its own. What happens to repeated keys differs by writer: S6.13, S6.14, S5.72. | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:140-162`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:96-117` | jsonout_duplicate_keys, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand_lenient, decstr_keys |

| S5.72 | **Writer difference 5:** repeated keys. Writer A prints every occurrence, in order, producing a JSON object with duplicate member names (`a: 1⏎a: 2⏎b: 3` → `{⏎  "a": 1.0,⏎  "a": 2.0,⏎  "b": 3.0⏎}`). Writer B never prints a repeated name: its input has gone through path expansion, which in strict mode rejects the repeat (S5.52) and with `--no-strict` keeps the LAST value at the FIRST position (`{⏎  "a": 2.0,⏎  "b": 3.0⏎}`). | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:140-162`, `src/decode/expand.rs:131-162` | jsonout_duplicate_keys, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand_lenient |
| S5.73 | **No other difference.** For every value that contains none of the 35 scalars of S5.68–S5.71 and no repeated key, the two writers produce identical bytes at every `N`: same shapes (S5.56–S5.61), same number text, same remaining escapes. The goldens pair up byte for byte: jsonout_indent_2 = jsonout_expand_indent_2. (Path expansion itself may change the VALUE when keys contain dots: that is the expansion extractor's subject, not a writer difference.) | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:22-208`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:42-120` | jsonout_indent_2, jsonout_expand_indent_2, decfmt_exp_compact_and_expand, decnum_expand_path |
| S5.74 | **Writer A as a relation on events** (library surface). Events are start-object, end-object, start-array, end-array, key, primitive; the `length` carried by start-array and the quoted flag carried by key are ignored. With `depth` = number of containers currently open: a primitive or a container start whose parent is an ARRAY is preceded by `,` when it is not the first element and then, when `N > 0`, by LF + `depth·N` spaces; one whose parent is an OBJECT, or that is the root, is preceded by nothing; a key is preceded by `,` when it is not the first key of its object and then, when `N > 0`, by LF + `depth·N` spaces, and is followed by `:` (`N = 0`) or `: ` (`N > 0`); an end event writes, when `N > 0` and the container received at least one key or element, LF + `(depth−1)·N` spaces, and then `}` or `]`. On every event sequence the decoder can produce this equals S5.56–S5.60. | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:22-208` | jsonout_indent_2, jsonout_root_array, toonedge_indent0_flat |
| S5.75 | **Writer A's own errors** (S9.77–S9.81) need a malformed event sequence. The decoder emits every start with its matching end inside one routine and follows every key with exactly one value (primitive or container), or aborts with its own error before the writer runs; so NO CLI input reaches these messages. The same holds for the tree builder that feeds writer B (S9.82). | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:57-61`, `src/cli/json_stream.rs:109-113`, `src/cli/json_stream.rs:141-147`, `src/cli/json_stream.rs:175-180`, `src/cli/json_stream.rs:201-205`, `src/decode/decoders.rs:30-177`, `src/decode/decoders.rs:369-500` | (case to add: none reachable from the CLI; a library-level stage golden needs a Rust driver, OQ-B3) |
| S5.76 | Malformed sequences that writer A does NOT detect (it writes them as they come): a key directly followed by another key; a container start inside an object without a preceding key; a second root value after the first. Unreachable from the CLI for the reason in S5.75. | `src/cli/json_stream.rs:33-55`, `src/cli/json_stream.rs:140-162` | (case to add: none reachable from the CLI, OQ-B3) |
| S5.77 | Writer B has no error path of its own: every value tree yields a text. Its recursion depth is bounded by the tree it is given, and that tree has passed path expansion, which has its own depth cap (message `Path expansion exceeded maximum depth of 256`, jsonout_expand_deep_limit; how that depth is counted is the expansion extractor's clause: oracle run, a plain 201-level nested object already exceeds it under `--expand-paths safe`, while writer A prints the same document without any limit). | `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:8-14`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:42-50`, `src/decode/expand.rs:11` | jsonout_expand_deep_limit, jsonout_expand_in_arrays |
| S5.78 | **What the CLI can reach in compact form.** With `N = 0` the TOON reader gives every line depth 0, so no line can be a child of another. Writer A at `N = 0` can therefore only print: a root primitive; a root array of primitives or `[]`; a root object whose member values are primitives, arrays of primitives, `{}` or `[]`. Writer B at `N = 0` can additionally print nested non-empty OBJECTS, built from dotted keys. A container inside an ARRAY is not reachable in compact form by either writer from the CLI; the compact rule S5.57 still defines it for library callers. | `src/decode/scanner.rs:133-137`, `src/cli/conversion.rs:44-57` | toonedge_indent0_flat, toonedge_indent0_indented_lenient, jsonout_root_array_compact jsonout_compact_nested_expand |

| S5.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5.30 | TOON number text is always of the form: optional `-`, an integer part (`0`, or digits not starting with `0`), and optionally `.` followed by digits the last of which is not `0`. It never contains an exponent, a `+`, a trailing `.0`, a leading `.`, or `-0`; `null` stands for no reachable number (S4.130). | `src/encode/primitives.rs:85` (Rust std `f64` `Display`) | encnum_ints, encnum_decimals, encnum_exponents, encnum_extremes |
| S5.31 | TOON layout. With digits d1…dn (S4.131, S4.132) and q the decimal exponent of d1: (a) q ≥ n − 1 (integer-valued): the digits followed by q − n + 1 zeros, no point: `1e21` → `1000000000000000000000`, `1.5e3` → `1500`, `1.0` → `1`; (b) 0 ≤ q < n − 1: the first q + 1 digits, `.`, the rest: `123456.789`, `900719925474099.3`; (c) q < 0: `0.`, then −q − 1 zeros, then the digits: `1e-7` → `0.0000001`, `1.5E-3` → `0.0015`. A negative value is the same text after `-`. | `src/encode/primitives.rs:92` (Rust std `f64` `Display`) | encnum_exponents, encnum_decimals, encnum_long_mantissa, encnum_pow10_slow_path, encnum_pow10_each, fx_enc_primitives_28, fx_enc_primitives_33, fx_enc_primitives_36 |

### S5, part D (the encoder: value to TOON lines, key folding): TOON output format (S5.1–S5.29)

Symbols used below: `I` = the indentation of the line (S4.61); `K` = a key in key-rule form (S4.21); `V` = a primitive's
text (S4.3–S4.5); `d` = the active delimiter character; `H` = `[N]`, `[N⇥]` or `[N\|]` (S4.24, S4.25, S4.27);
`F` = `{K1 d K2 d …}` without spaces (S4.26); `J` = `V1 d V2 d …` without spaces (S4.28).

| S5.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5.1 | Success output. stdout receives the lines joined with single U+000A bytes, then ONE final U+000A. A zero-line document (the empty root object, S4.60) gives exactly one byte, `\n`. stderr is empty and the exit code is 0. The final `\n` is written whether or not the input ended with a newline. U+000D is never written as a line terminator. | `src/cli/mod.rs:156-191` | fx_enc_whitespace_01, fx_enc_objects_03, encstr_no_trailing_newline, encstr_whitespace_json, stats_empty_object |
| S5.2 | No line ends with U+0020 and no line is empty, by construction: a value text is never empty (the empty string is `""`); an empty inline array is the header alone with no space after the colon; an empty-object list item is `-` without a space; `K:` lines end at the colon. No blank line separates anything. The only "empty" output is the zero-line document of S5.1. | `src/encode/encoders.rs:250-252`, `src/encode/encoders.rs:363-366`, `src/encode/encoders.rs:518-530` | fx_enc_arrays_primitive_04, fx_enc_arrays_objects_14, fx_enc_objects_26, fx_enc_arrays_primitive_05, enc_shapes_mixed |
| S5.3 | Closed set of line shapes. Every line of every document has exactly one of these shapes: (1) `V` (root primitive; depth 0 only); (2) `I K: V`; (3) `I K:`; (4) `I K H:` or `I H:` (empty array, list-form header); (5) `I K H: J` or `I H: J` (inline array); (6) `I K H F:` or `I H F:` (tabular header); (7) `I J` (tabular row); (8) `I - V`; (9) `I -`; (10) `I - H: J` or `I - H:` (array as list item); (11) `I - K: V`, `I - K:`, `I - K H: J`, `I - K H:`, `I - K H F:` (first field of a list-item object). In every shape `: ` is a colon and ONE space, `- ` is a hyphen and ONE space, and K is glued to H with nothing between. | `src/encode/encoders.rs:486-618`, `src/encode/primitives.rs:52-83` | enc_shapes_mixed, fx_enc_arrays_objects_06, fx_enc_arrays_nested_08, fx_enc_primitives_01 |
| S5.4 | Indentation `I` is `indent × depth` U+0020 characters and nothing else; a TAB never appears in indentation, only as the tab delimiter inside H, F and J. | `src/encode/encoders.rs:486-496` | fx_enc_whitespace_02, fx_enc_whitespace_03, fx_enc_delimiters_04 |
| S5.5 | The three delimiters as they appear: comma: `[N]`, `{a,b}`, `1,2`; tab: `[N⇥]`, `{a⇥b}`, `1⇥2`; pipe: `[N\|]`, `{a\|b}`, `1\|2`. The marker is inside the brackets of EVERY header of the document, also of headers whose array uses no delimiter (list form, empty). | `src/encode/primitives.rs:64-79`, `src/encode/primitives.rs:42-46` | fx_enc_delimiters_01, fx_enc_delimiters_02, fx_enc_delimiters_03, fx_enc_delimiters_04, fx_enc_delimiters_05, fx_enc_delimiters_06, fx_enc_delimiters_07, flag_delimiter_word_comma, flag_delimiter_word_pipe, flag_delimiter_backslash_t, flag_delimiter_real_tab |
| S5.6 | Quoted text is `"`, body, `"` where the body contains exactly five possible escapes (`\\`, `\"`, `\n`, `\r`, `\t`) and every other scalar value raw (S4.19). A quoted text never spans lines. There is no single-quote form and no `\u` form. | `src/shared/string_utils.rs:4-32`, `src/encode/primitives.rs:24` | fx_enc_primitives_11, fx_enc_primitives_14, fx_enc_objects_07, encstr_escapes_in |
| S5.7 | Output encoding is UTF-8 with no byte-order mark added. Non-ASCII scalar values are written as themselves (never escaped), in bare and quoted texts alike. Control characters other than U+0009, U+000A, U+000D are written as raw bytes (S10.60). | `src/cli/mod.rs:183-185`, `src/shared/string_utils.rs:28` | encstr_unicode, encstr_escapes_in, fx_enc_primitives_23, fx_enc_primitives_24, fx_enc_primitives_25, fx_enc_primitives_26 |
| S5.8 | Type distinguishability. `null`, `true`, `false` and numbers are never quoted; a STRING that is exactly `true`, `false`, `null` or numeric-like (S4.10, S4.11) is always quoted, in every position and under every delimiter. | `src/encode/primitives.rs:10-25`, `src/shared/validation.rs:56-58` | fx_enc_delimiters_22, fx_enc_arrays_primitive_09, fx_enc_arrays_tabular_04, fx_enc_primitives_07, fx_enc_objects_10, fx_enc_objects_11 |
| S5.9 | Lines are never wrapped, truncated, padded or aligned: a 5000-character string gives the single line `long: xxx…x` of 5006 characters, a 300-character key gives `kkk…k: v` of 303 characters, a row is as long as its cells. | `src/encode/encoders.rs:499-515` | encstr_long, large_tabular_1500 |
| S5.10 | Folded output shapes are ordinary shapes whose K contains dots: `a.b.c: 1` (shape 2), `a.b.c:` (shape 3, for both an empty-object leaf and a partial fold), `a.b[2]: 1,2` (5), `a.b.items[2]{id,name}:` (6), `a.b[2]:` (4). | `src/encode/encoders.rs:101-146` | fx_enc_key_folding_01, fx_enc_key_folding_02, fx_enc_key_folding_03, fx_enc_key_folding_06, fx_enc_key_folding_11, fx_enc_key_folding_12 |
| S5.11 | Determinism: the same input bytes and flags give the same output bytes on every run. No clock, locale, environment variable, random source or hash order reaches the document (S6.34). | `src/encode/encoders.rs:16-34` | determinism_a, determinism_a_again |
| S5.12 | The document bytes of S5.1 are the same with `--stats` and with `-o FILE` (where they go to the file); those flags only add stderr lines, specified in the CLI part. Flags that belong to decoding (`--no-strict`, `--expand-paths safe`) are accepted in encode mode and change nothing. | `src/cli/mod.rs:49-52`, `src/cli/mod.rs:139-154` | stats_small, stats_empty_object, flag_output_dev_stdout_encode, flag_no_strict_on_encode_ignored, flag_expand_paths_on_encode_ignored |

## S8. Effect inventory

### S8, part A (command line, effects, I/O errors, --stats): Effect inventory (the shell's complete effect list)

Order of effects in one run: S8.1 → (argv error or help/version: S8.3, S8.4, S8.13 and exit) → S8.5 or S8.6 → S8.7 → S8.8 (pure) → S8.9/S8.10 or S8.11+S8.12 → S8.14 (statistics, then success line) → S8.15. Nothing else happens.

| S8.n | effect | when | failure behavior | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S8.1 | read argv as OS byte strings (argv[0] only for `<prog>`, S1.2) | once, at start | cannot fail; non-UTF-8 words per S1.22 | mod.rs:20; clap:builder/command.rs:902 | usage_help, usage_unknown_flag, flag_short_combined_e |
| S8.2 | no application environment variable, no configuration file, no current-directory lookup other than resolving the typed relative paths, no locale, no clock, no randomness, no network, no child process, no thread, no temporary file, no file deletion or rename, no lock, no fsync | never | not applicable; repeated runs are byte-identical (floor check STABLE) | mod.rs:19-27; args.rs:15-59 | determinism_a, determinism_a_again, happy_readme_users |
| S8.3 | read environment `NO_COLOR`, `CLICOLOR_FORCE`, `CLICOLOR`, `TERM`, `CI` | only when clap prints the help text or an argv error (S1.21) | absent variables mean plain text when the stream is not a terminal | anstream:auto.rs:198-222 | usage_help, usage_unknown_flag |
| S8.4 | terminal test (isatty) of the stream about to receive clap's text (stdout for help, stderr for argv errors) | same moment as S8.3 | none | anstream:auto.rs:212-216 | usage_help, usage_unknown_flag |
| S8.5 | read stdin to end of file into memory | only when argv was accepted and INPUT is absent or `-`; never for help, version or argv errors; never when INPUT is a path (stdin is then left untouched) | OS read error → S9.12; invalid UTF-8 → S9.7; a missing fd 0 reads as empty | mod.rs:115-133 | auto_stdin_defaults_encode, flag_stdin_dash, flag_stdin_dash_decode, usage_none, usage_conflict_encode_decode |
| S8.6 | open INPUT path read-only, read it whole, close it; the path is used as typed (relative to the working directory, symlinks followed, special files allowed) | only when argv was accepted and INPUT is a path; before any output is opened | S9.4, S9.5, S9.6; invalid UTF-8 → S9.8 | mod.rs:135-137 | auto_json_ext, auto_toon_ext, flag_double_dash_file, usage_missing_file, usage_dir_as_input |
| S8.7 | strict UTF-8 validation of the complete input, no BOM removal, no newline translation, no trimming | immediately after the read, before any parsing | S9.7/S9.8; wins over every parse error (S9.10) | mod.rs:128-131, 136 | jsonerr_invalid_utf8_stdin, jsonerr_invalid_utf8_file, toonerr_invalid_utf8_stdin, jsonerr_overlong_utf8, jsonerr_truncated_utf8 |
| S8.8 | conversion (encode or decode) is a pure function of the input text and the option values; it completes entirely in memory before the first output byte is written | after S8.7 | its failures are S9.1/S9.3; no output exists yet | conversion.rs:17-42; mod.rs:46, 100 | happy_readme_users, happy_readme_users_decode, happy_config_folded, happy_config_expanded, auto_toon_content_json_ext_fails |
| S8.9 | write stdout, encode: each TOON line, LF between lines, final LF (S5.100); stdout is line-buffered, so everything through the final LF has reached the descriptor when the call returns | no `-o` | S9.16 | mod.rs:148-151, 176-188 | happy_readme_users, determinism_a, stats_small |
| S8.10 | write stdout, decode: each JSON piece, then LF (S5.101) | no `-o` | S9.16 | mod.rs:208-217 | happy_readme_users_decode, flag_short_d |
| S8.11 | create-or-truncate the `-o` path for writing (write-only, create, truncate; default permissions; symlinks followed; special files such as `/dev/stdout`, `/dev/stderr`, `/dev/null` allowed) | with `-o`, after a successful conversion, before writing | S9.13 | mod.rs:141, 158, 195 | flag_output_dev_stdout_encode, flag_output_dev_stdout_decode, usage_output_bad_dir |
| S8.12 | write the `-o` file: without `--stats` (both modes) through an 8192-byte buffer that is flushed when the file is closed, *ignoring* a flush error; with `--stats` (encode) as two direct writes (text, then LF) | after S8.11 | S9.15 with the S10.3 exception | mod.rs:141-146, 159-174, 196-206 | flag_output_dev_stdout_encode, flag_output_file_label, stats_with_output |
| S8.13 | clap prints help/version to stdout (exit 0) or an argv error to stderr (exit 2); a write failure is ignored | at the scan position of S1.15/S1.19 | none (S1.63, S1.70) | clap:error/mod.rs:245-248 | usage_help, usage_version, usage_unknown_flag |
| S8.14 | write stderr lines, unbuffered, in this order: statistics (S5.108), success line (S5.105/S5.106); or the single runtime error line | after all output bytes were written (or at the failure) | a failed stderr write aborts the process (S9.20) | mod.rs:65-69, 76-80, 106-110; main.rs:4 | stats_small, stats_with_output, flag_output_dev_stdout_encode, usage_missing_file |
| S8.15 | exit: 0 by normal return after success; 1 right after printing a runtime error; 2/0 from clap | end | none | main.rs:1-7 | happy_readme_users, usage_none, usage_unknown_flag, usage_help |
| S8.16 | signal disposition: SIGPIPE is ignored (the runtime's default), so a closed stdout reader surfaces as the S9.16 message with exit 1 instead of a silent signal death | whole run | S9.16 | main.rs:1 | flag_short_combined_e, (case to add: none expressible — needs a harness that closes the stdout reader; oracle run in the report) |
| S8.17 | memory: input text, the full converted output (all TOON lines or all JSON pieces) and, with `--stats`, a second joined copy of the TOON text are held at once; nothing is streamed from input to output | whole run | out-of-memory is outside this spec | mod.rs:46-51, 100-103 | determinism_a, stats_ratio_5 |


## S9. Error and edge behavior

### S9, part A (command line, effects, I/O errors, --stats): Error and edge behavior (CLI and I/O rows)

Argv errors (exit 2) are S1.70–S1.87. Every row below is a runtime row: stderr, exit 1, framing S5.111, unless it says otherwise.

| S9.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S9.1 | JSON parse failure (encode mode): stderr `JSON error: Failed to parse JSON: <m>\n`, where `<m>` is the JSON reader's own message ending in ` at line L column C` (owned by the JSON-input part); exit 1; stdout empty; with `-o` the file is neither created nor truncated; with `--stats` no statistics lines. Worked example (68 bytes): `JSON error: Failed to parse JSON: expected ident at line 1 column 2\n`. | error.rs:33, 218-220; conversion.rs:21-22 | auto_toon_content_json_ext_fails, usage_none, stats_json_error |
| S9.2 | No arguments with an empty stdin (the harness connects `/dev/null`): encode mode parses the empty text → stderr `JSON error: Failed to parse JSON: EOF while parsing a value at line 1 column 0\n` (79 bytes), exit 1. There is no "missing input" usage error and no help text. The same line results from an empty `.json` file, from `/dev/null` as INPUT and from a closed stdin [run]. | mod.rs:29-31; args.rs:109-110 | usage_none, auto_empty_json_file |
| S9.3 | TOON decode failure: stderr is the decoder's message verbatim + LF with no prefix (the README's `Failed to decode TOON:` prefix does not exist, OQ-006); exit 1; stdout empty because the conversion finishes before any output byte (S8.8). Worked example: `Line 3: Blank lines inside list array are not allowed in strict mode\n`. The message texts are owned by the decode part. | error.rs:8-21; main.rs:3-5 | toonerr_blank_in_list, toonerr_extra_list_items |
| S9.4 | INPUT path does not exist (also when a directory component is missing): stderr `Failed to read file '<path>': No such file or directory (os error 2)\n`, exit 1, identical in both modes. Worked example (106 bytes): `Failed to read file 'cases/inputs/hand/files/does-not-exist.json': No such file or directory (os error 2)\n`. After the escape, flag-looking words are such paths: `-- --help` → `Failed to read file '--help': …`. | error.rs:24, 176-178; mod.rs:135-137 | usage_missing_file, usage_missing_file_toon, usage_double_dash_help_is_path, usage_word_help_is_a_path |
| S9.5 | INPUT is a directory: stderr `Failed to read file '<path>': Is a directory (os error 21)\n`, exit 1. Worked example (76 bytes): `Failed to read file 'cases/inputs/hand/files': Is a directory (os error 21)\n`. | error.rs:176-178 | usage_dir_as_input |
| S9.6 | Other INPUT read failures use the same template `Failed to read file '<path>': <os text> (os error <n>)\n` [run]: no read permission → `Permission denied (os error 13)`; a regular file used as a directory (`plain.json/`) → `Not a directory (os error 20)`. | error.rs:176-178 | usage_missing_file, io_input_trailing_slash |
| S9.7 | Invalid UTF-8 on stdin, either mode: stderr `Failed to read stdin: stream did not contain valid UTF-8\n` (57 bytes), exit 1. No byte offset, no `(os error …)` part. | error.rs:194-196; mod.rs:127-133 | jsonerr_invalid_utf8_stdin, toonerr_invalid_utf8_stdin, jsonerr_overlong_utf8, jsonerr_truncated_utf8 |
| S9.8 | Invalid UTF-8 in the INPUT file: stderr `Failed to read file '<path>': stream did not contain valid UTF-8\n`, exit 1. | error.rs:176-178; mod.rs:135-137 | jsonerr_invalid_utf8_file |
| S9.9 | "Invalid UTF-8" means any byte sequence that is not well-formed UTF-8: a continuation byte without a lead, a lead byte without all its continuations (also when cut off by end of input), overlong forms (leads C0, C1; E0 followed by 80–9F; F0 followed by 80–8F), surrogates (ED followed by A0–BF), values above U+10FFFF (F4 followed by 90–BF; leads F5–FF). Nothing is replaced or skipped. NUL and every other control character are valid [run: `ED A0 80`, `F5 80 80 80`, a late `FF`, `a: x\x00y` decodes]. | mod.rs:128-131 (Rust `read_to_string`) | jsonerr_overlong_utf8, jsonerr_truncated_utf8, jsonerr_invalid_utf8_stdin, jsonerr_invalid_utf8_file |
| S9.10 | The complete input is read and validated before parsing starts: one invalid byte anywhere (after a syntax error, in trailing whitespace) gives S9.7/S9.8 and never a parse error [run: `{bad \xff` and `a: "x\n\xff` → S9.7]. | mod.rs:31, 87, 128-131 | jsonerr_invalid_utf8_stdin, toonerr_invalid_utf8_stdin, io_utf8_error_beats_json_error |
| S9.11 | Not I/O errors at this layer: a UTF-8 BOM is not removed (the JSON reader then fails with `expected value at line 1 column 1`); CRLF and lone CR pass through unchanged to the parsers; an input without a final newline is fine [run]. | mod.rs:115-137 | flag_short_combined_e, io_bom_json_file |
| S9.12 | stdin cannot be read (for example stdin is a directory) [run]: stderr `Failed to read stdin: Is a directory (os error 21)\n`, exit 1; template `Failed to read stdin: <os text> (os error <n>)\n`. A process started without fd 0 sees an empty stdin (then S9.2 in encode mode, `{}` in decode mode). | error.rs:194-196; mod.rs:127-133 | usage_none, (case to add: none expressible — the harness only supports regular-file stdin; oracle run in the report) |
| S9.13 | `-o` path cannot be created: stderr `Failed to create file '<path>': <os text> (os error <n>)\n`, exit 1, stdout empty, no statistics, no success line. Worked example (103 bytes): `Failed to create file 'cases/inputs/hand/no-such-dir/out.toon': No such file or directory (os error 2)\n`. Also [run]: the path is a directory → `Is a directory (os error 21)`; no permission → `Permission denied (os error 13)`. | error.rs:188-190; mod.rs:141, 158, 195 | usage_output_bad_dir, io_output_is_dir, stats_output_bad_dir |
| S9.14 | Order between input-side and output-side failures: reading, UTF-8 validation and conversion all precede the creation of the `-o` file, so with a bad input *and* an uncreatable `-o` path the input-side message wins (`{bad` with `-o nodir/x` → S9.1; missing INPUT with `-o nodir/x` → S9.4; invalid UTF-8 → S9.7) [run]. | mod.rs:29-51, 85-103 | usage_output_bad_dir, io_json_error_beats_bad_output |
| S9.15 | Write failure on the `-o` file: stderr `Failed to write to file '<path>': <os text> (os error <n>)\n`, exit 1, no success line [run with `/dev/full`: `No space left on device (os error 28)`]. In ENCODE mode with `--stats` every failed write is reported. In every other situation (encode without `--stats`, and decode with or without `--stats`, which decode ignores by S1.153) the failure is reported only when the output exceeds 8192 bytes (S10.3) [run, pass 2: `printf 'a: 1\n' \| toon -d --stats -o /dev/full` → success line, exit 0]. | error.rs:182-184; mod.rs:142-146, 163-174, 199-206 | flag_output_dev_stdout_encode, io_output_dev_full_stats, io_output_dev_full_8193, io_output_dev_full_decode_stats, io_output_dev_full_decode_8193, io_output_dev_full_lines_8193 |
| S9.16 | stdout write failure: stderr `Failed to write to stdout: <os text> (os error <n>)\n`, exit 1 [run: stdout = `/dev/full` → `No space left on device (os error 28)`; stdout = a pipe whose reader is closed → `Broken pipe (os error 32)`, the process is not killed by SIGPIPE]. With `--stats` no statistics lines follow. Bytes already delivered stay delivered. | error.rs:200-202; mod.rs:148-151, 176-188, 208-217 | flag_short_combined_e, (case to add: none expressible — needs stdout redirection in the harness; oracle run in the report) |
| S9.17 | OS error text: `<description> (os error <errno>)`, description and number from the platform (Linux values observed: 2 `No such file or directory`, 13 `Permission denied`, 20 `Not a directory`, 21 `Is a directory`, 28 `No space left on device`, 32 `Broken pipe`). The UTF-8 failure of S9.7/S9.8 is not an OS error and has no parenthesis. Producing these texts on every lane is OQ-008. | error.rs:24 | usage_missing_file, usage_dir_as_input, usage_output_bad_dir, jsonerr_invalid_utf8_stdin |
| S9.18 | Paths inside messages: the word exactly as typed (no absolute path, no normalization), between ASCII single quotes, unescaped (a quote, space or LF in the path is copied raw), invalid UTF-8 bytes replaced by U+FFFD [run]. stdin messages carry no path. | error.rs:24 | usage_missing_file, usage_dir_as_input, usage_output_bad_dir, jsonerr_invalid_utf8_file |
| S9.19 | Partial output: every failure before writing (S9.1–S9.14) leaves stdout empty and the `-o` file untouched. A write failure (S9.15/S9.16) may leave a truncated output (the `-o` file was already truncated by S8.11). | mod.rs:139-220 | usage_none, usage_output_bad_dir, auto_toon_content_json_ext_fails |
| S9.20 | A FAILING stderr write (`/dev/full`) when a runtime line of S8.14 is printed aborts the process (SIGABRT, status 134 in a shell, −6 for a parent) [run]; stdout bytes already written stay. A CLOSED stderr (`2>&-`) is different: the write error is ignored and the exit code is the normal one (corrected in round 6) [run: `-e one.json -o /dev/null 2>&-` → exit 0; a missing file with `2>&-` → exit 1]. Argv errors and help/version are exempt either way (their write failure is ignored: exit 2 / 0). | main.rs:4; mod.rs:65-79; Cargo.toml:84 | stats_small, (case to add: none expressible — needs stderr redirection in the harness; oracle run in the report) |
| S9.21 | Edge, not an error: empty input in decode mode (empty stdin, empty `.toon` file) succeeds with stdout `{}\n`, exit 0 [run]. | mod.rs:85-103 | flag_short_d, auto_empty_toon_file |
| S9.22 | Unreachable texts present in the source and never printed by this program: `No input file specified` (INPUT absent always means stdin) and the `I/O error: …` prefix (no caller). A port need not contain them. | mod.rs:122; error.rs:229-232 | auto_stdin_defaults_encode, usage_none |

### S9, part B (JSON text input and the two JSON writers): Error and edge behavior — JSON input errors, one row per message (S9.50–S9.99)

Rows S9.50–S9.68 are the nineteen serde_json messages that `from_str::<Value>` can produce. Each is delivered exactly as S2.46 says: stderr, one line
`JSON error: Failed to parse JSON: <message> at line L column C` + LF; exit code 1; stdout empty; no output file. `L:C` comes from the error index `i`
through the position function S2.40. Rows S9.69–S9.74 are the remaining serde_json error codes, which this call path cannot produce. Messages that
contain a backtick are shown inside double-backtick code spans: the backticks around the punctuation are literal U+0060 characters of the message.

| S9.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S9.50 | **`EOF while parsing a value`** — stderr, exit 1, stdout empty. Trigger: end of input (a) where a value must start: empty or whitespace-only input, right after a `:`, right after a `,` inside an array OR an object; (b) inside a literal (`t`, `tru`, `nul`, `fals`); (c) right after a number's `-`; (d) right after a number's `.`; (e) right after a number's `e`/`E` or exponent sign. Position: `i = len` (S2.42). Examples: empty 1:0; one space 1:1; `⏎` 2:0; `  ⏎⇥⏎` 3:0; ` ⇥␍⏎  ` 2:2; `␍` 1:1; `tru` 1:3; `⏎⏎  fals` 3:6; `{"é":nul` 1:9; `-` 1:1; `⏎-` 2:1; `[-` 1:2; `1.` 1:2; `-1.` 1:3; `["é",1.` 1:8; `1e` 1:2; `1e+` 1:3; `1.5e` 1:4; `0e` 1:2; `[1,` 1:3; `[1,⏎` 2:0; `{"a":1,` 1:7; `{"a":1,⏎  ` 2:2; `{"a":` 1:5; `{"a":⏎` 2:0; `{"😀":` 1:8. | `de.rs:1397-1402`, `de.rs:448-450`, `de.rs:463-468`, `de.rs:553-557`, `de.rs:587-592`, `de.rs:1957`, `de.rs:2013`, `serde_json-1.0.151/src/error.rs:357` | jsonerr_empty, jsonerr_whitespace, jsonerr_bad_true, jsonerr_bad_null, jsonerr_lone_minus, jsonerr_dot_end, jsonerr_exp_end, jsonerr_eof_after_colon jsonerr_eof_after_comma_arr, jsonerr_eof_after_comma_obj, jsonerr_exp_sign_end |
| S9.51 | **`EOF while parsing a string`** — stderr, exit 1, stdout empty. Trigger: end of input (a) before a string's closing quote; (b) right after a backslash; (c) with fewer than four bytes left after `\u` (whatever they are, S2.19); (d) where the `\` or the `u` of the second surrogate escape must be (S2.23). Position: `i = len`. ### S9, part C (numbers: four text/value algorithms and their glue): Error and edge behavior — S9.200–S9.219 (number-related)

Every JSON error below has the shape: stderr receives exactly `JSON error: Failed to parse JSON: <message> at line L column C` followed by a newline; stdout stays empty; exit code 1; nothing of the document is encoded. L and C are defined in S9.203.

| S9.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S9.200 | Trigger: a number literal whose multiplication F0 × P(T) (S4.113) is infinite. Message `number out of range`, stderr, exit 1, stdout empty. Position: the last byte of the whole literal (the literal is consumed completely before the multiplication). Examples: `{"a":1e309}` is NOT this path (S9.201); `9e308` → `… at line 1 column 5`; `1.7976931348623158e308` → column 22; `{…"n17":1.7976931348623158e308,…}` → `JSON error: Failed to parse JSON: number out of range at line 1 column 308`. | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:651`, `serde_json-1.0.151/src/error.rs:366`, `src/error.rs:33`, `src/error.rs:219`, `src/main.rs:4` | encnum_mixed_digits |
| S9.201 | Trigger: T > 308 with F0 ≠ 0 (S4.116). Same message, stream and exit code as S9.200; position: the last byte of the literal. `{"a":1e309}` → `JSON error: Failed to parse JSON: number out of range at line 1 column 10`; `{"a":-1e309}` → column 11; oracle: `1e2147483647` in `{"a":1e2147483647}` → column 17; an integer of 401 digits → the column of its last digit (a 310-digit integer reports the same column through S9.200). | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:664` | encnum_overflow_positive, encnum_overflow_negative |
| S9.202 | Trigger: an exponent digit that overflows X (S4.107) while S ≠ 0 and the exponent is not negative. Same message, stream and exit code; position: the overflowing digit itself (it has been consumed; the digits after it have not), so the column points INTO the literal. `{"a":1e99999999999999999999}` → `JSON error: Failed to parse JSON: number out of range at line 1 column 17` (the 10th exponent digit; the literal ends at column 27). Trailing garbage after that digit is never reached: oracle `1e99999999999999999999x` → column 12. | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:604`, `de.rs:866` | encnum_overflow_huge_exponent |
| S9.203 | Position arithmetic for all JSON errors on numbers: the position is a byte index i into the input; L = 1 + the count of LF bytes before i; C = i minus the index just after the last LF before i (so C counts BYTES consumed on that line, a multi-byte character counts as its byte length, and CR is an ordinary byte). "At the last consumed byte" means i = bytes consumed so far; "at the next byte" means one more, capped at the input length. Oracle: `{` LF `  "a": 1e309` LF `}` → `line 2 column 12`; `{"a":[1,` LF `2,` LF `  1e99999999999999999999]}` → `line 3 column 14`; `{"é":1e309}` → `line 1 column 11`. | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/read.rs:421`, `read.rs:573`, `read.rs:577`, `serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:241`, `de.rs:248` | encnum_overflow_positive, jsonerr_second_line_error, jsonerr_unicode_in_error_col encnum_exp_overflow_multiline, encnum_error_column_bytes |
| S9.204 | Trigger: integer part `0` immediately followed by a digit. Message `invalid number`, at the next byte (the second digit). `01` → `JSON error: Failed to parse JSON: invalid number at line 1 column 2`; oracle: `-01` → column 3; `00` → column 2. | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:474` | jsonerr_leading_zero |
| S9.205 | Trigger: `.` not followed by a digit. When a byte follows: `invalid number` at that byte (oracle: `1.x` → column 3; `[2.]` → column 4). At end of input: `EOF while parsing a value` at the capped next-byte position (`1.` → `… at line 1 column 2`; oracle `0.` → column 2; after an overflowed integer part too: `18446744073709551616.` → column 21). | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:553` | jsonerr_dot_end |
| S9.206 | Trigger: `e`/`E` (and optional sign) not followed by a digit. At end of input: `EOF while parsing a value` at the last consumed byte (`1e` → column 2; oracle `1e+` → column 3, `1.0e` → column 4). Otherwise the offending byte is consumed and the message is `invalid number` at that byte (oracle: `1ex` → column 3; `1e+x` → column 4). | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:587`, `de.rs:598` | jsonerr_exp_end |
| S9.207 | Trigger: `-` not followed by a digit. At end of input: `EOF while parsing a value` at the last consumed byte (`-` → column 1). Otherwise the byte after `-` is consumed and the message is `invalid number` there (`-x` → column 2; oracle: `- 1` → column 2; `-Infinity` → column 2). | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:463`, `de.rs:505` | jsonerr_lone_minus, jsonerr_minus_word |
| S9.208 | A value cannot start with `+`, `.`, or a letter other than the three literals: message `expected value` at the first byte of the value (`+1`, `.5`, `NaN`, `Infinity` → `… expected value at line 1 column 1`). These are not number errors but mark the edge of the number grammar. | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/error.rs:362` | jsonerr_plus, jsonerr_dot_start, jsonerr_nan, jsonerr_infinity |
| S9.209 | A number literal ends at the first byte that cannot continue it; what follows is judged by the surrounding structure, not by the number reader. At the root: `trailing characters` at that byte (oracle: `1.5.5` → column 4; `0x10` → column 2; `1e5e5` → column 4; `1_000` → column 2; `1 2` → column 3; `0e99999999999999999999x` → column 23). Inside an array: oracle `[1.5.5]` → ``expected `,` or `]` at line 1 column 5``. | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:148` | jsonerr_trailing_chars |
| S9.210 | Trigger (decode): an array header whose parsed length N > 100000000. stderr receives exactly `Declared array length N exceeds maximum allowed (100000000)` and a newline (no `Line` prefix, N in plain decimal), stdout empty, exit 1; identical under `--no-strict`. | `src/decode/parser.rs:108` | toonerr_length_over_cap, toonerr_huge_length, toonlenient_huge_length |
| S9.211 | Not errors (decode): a malformed length (`[abc]`, `[-1]`, `[]`, `[1.5]`, `[ 2]`, a value above 18446744073709551615) makes the line an ordinary key-value line, exit 0 (S4.190); a numeric-looking token that fails S4.141 or S4.142 is a string, exit 0 (S4.140). The decode side has no number-format error of its own. | `src/decode/parser.rs:101`, `src/decode/parser.rs:274` | toonerr_bad_length, toonerr_negative_length, toonerr_empty_length, toonerr_float_length, decnum_not_numbers, decnum_extremes |
| S9.212 | Not errors (encode): zero with any exponent (`0e99999999999999999999` → `0`), any nonzero literal with a hugely negative exponent (`1e-99999999999999999999` → `0`), any literal that underflows (`1e-400` → `0`), and any number of digits in any part of a literal. | `serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:866`, `de.rs:660` | encnum_zero_huge_exponent, encnum_tiny_huge_exponent, encnum_extremes, encnum_frac_long |

### S9, part E (the decoder: TOON lines to events and values, path expansion): Error and edge behavior — decode errors (S9.100–S9.199)

Every row: the message is written to **stderr** followed by one LF, the exit code is **1**, and **stdout is empty**
at that moment (the JSON text is produced only after a complete successful decode, S4.200; with `-o FILE` the file
is not created). There is no `Failed to decode TOON:` prefix (OQ-006). `N`, `M`, `L` are decimal integers without
padding or separators.

|---|---|---|---|

Stream: stderr, followed by one LF; exit code 1; stdout empty. | `src/decode/scanner.rs:85` | toonedge_indent0_indented, jsonout_expand_indent_0 |

| S9.140 | **Precedence 1 — scanner first.** In strict mode any S9.100–S9.102 on ANY line beats every other decode or expansion error (S2.111). | S9.141 | **Precedence 2 — one structural pass.** After scanning, lines are consumed top to bottom; the first error met is reported. | S9.145 | **Precedence 6 — list body:** errors raised inside an item (its tokens, its nested arrays with all their checks) come first, in item order; then S9.112; then S9.114; then S9.113. | S9.147 | **Precedence 8 — expansion last:** S9.116–S9.118 can only appear for a document that decodes without error (S4.249); among them the first in the depth-first, document-order walk of S4.242 wins.
