### S10, part A (command line, effects, I/O errors, --stats): Known bugs and oddities on this surface (status: Amendment A3)

| S10.n | behavior | why it is a bug | reproduce with case | decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S10.1 | `--indent 99 --help` prints the range error, exit 2 (S1.131); help wins only when it comes first (clap:parser/parser.rs:1150) | the corpus case is *named* "help wins over bad flag value" and its golden shows the opposite; the name is wrong, the golden is right | usage_help_wins_over_bad_flag_value; usage_help_first_wins | bug-compatible; suggest renaming the case |
| S10.2 | `-o -` creates a file literally named `-` (mod.rs:141), while INPUT `-` means stdin (args.rs:116) | asymmetric; users expect `-` to mean stdout | flag_stdin_dash for the input side; (case to add: none — the run writes into the working directory; oracle run in the report) | bug-compatible |
| S10.3 | Without `--stats`, a write error on the `-o` file is lost when the whole output is at most 8192 bytes: exit 0 and the success line is printed although nothing was stored; at 8193 bytes or more the error S9.15 appears. In encode mode with `--stats` the error is always reported; decode mode never takes that path (mod.rs:159, 196: buffered writer dropped without an explicit flush) [run: `-e -o /dev/full`; pass 2: the total decides, not the line sizes (910 short lines), and the decode path behaves the same through both JSON writers and with `--indent 0`] | a success report for a failed write | io_output_dev_full_8192, io_output_dev_full_8193, io_output_dev_full_stats, io_output_dev_full_decode_8192, io_output_dev_full_decode_8193, io_output_dev_full_decode_expand_8192, io_output_dev_full_decode_expand_8193, io_output_dev_full_decode_stats, io_output_dev_full_lines_8192, io_output_dev_full_lines_8193 | bug-compatible by default; candidate BugFix DISC (OQ-A6) |
| S10.4 | `Saved ~1 tokens` — the noun is never singular (mod.rs:68) | grammar | stats_tie_to_even_down | bug-compatible |
| S10.5 | README CLI example shows `# Token estimates: ~1250 (JSON) -> ~520 (TOON)` with an ASCII arrow and no preceding empty line; the program prints U+2192 and an empty first line (mod.rs:65-66) | README disagrees with goldens | stats_small | golden wins |
| S10.6 | README shows decode output as one compact line with integer ids (`{"users":[{"id":1,…}]}`); the program prints indented JSON (indent 2 by default) with `1.0` (mod.rs:91; OQ-007) | README disagrees with goldens | happy_readme_users_decode | golden wins (number form: DISC candidate C-1) |
| S10.7 | README lists the delimiter spellings as `,`, backslash-`t`, backslash-pipe; the program rejects backslash-pipe and additionally accepts `comma`, `tab`, `pipe`, the bare pipe character and a real TAB (args.rs:73-82) | README disagrees with the program | flag_delimiter_word_pipe, flag_delimiter_equals_pipe, usage_delimiter_backslash_pipe | golden wins |
| S10.8 | README says "CLI wiring is in progress" and writes the usage as `toon [options] [input]`; the CLI is complete and prints `toon [OPTIONS] [INPUT]` (args.rs:5-59) | stale README | usage_help | golden wins |
| S10.9 | The help text does not state the 0..=16 range of `--indent`, nor the word spellings `comma`/`tab`/`pipe`; its first line comes from the crate description, not from the struct's doc comment `TOON CLI — Convert between JSON and TOON formats` (args.rs:4-6, 32-38; Cargo.toml:6) | undocumented limits | usage_help, usage_bad_indent_17, flag_delimiter_word_tab | bug-compatible |
| S10.10 | `--indent` values are read as 64-bit signed before the range check, so `256` and `-1` report `… is not in 0..=16` while only values beyond ±2^63 report `number too large/small to fit in target type` (clap:builder/value_parser.rs:1418-1438) | the 8-bit type never produces its own overflow text | usage_bad_indent_256, usage_bad_indent_huge, usage_bad_indent_negative | bug-compatible |
| S10.11 | `--indent -1` (two words) is "unexpected argument '-1'" while `--indent=-1` is the range error; `--indent=-0` is accepted as 0 (clap:parser/parser.rs:213-282) | the same value gives two different errors depending on spelling | usage_bad_indent_negative, usage_negative_number_word, usage_indent_minus_zero | bug-compatible |
| S10.12 | A flag written with `=value` after any committed argument prints the one-line group usage G listing every argument between `<` and `>` and omitting `[INPUT]` (S1.86; clap:parser/parser.rs:829-846 keeps the derive group in the list) | an unreadable usage line produced by a library quirk | usage_flag_with_value_after_commit | bug-compatible |
| S10.13 | An unknown option or extra positional directly after a pending item hides that item's error (S1.132; clap:parser/parser.rs:187, 271, 474) | the leftmost problem is not the one reported | usage_unknown_long_swallows_bad_value, usage_extra_positional_swallows_bad_value | bug-compatible |
| S10.14 | The empty word as INPUT is an argv error naming `[INPUT]` (exit 2) instead of a file error (clap:builder/value_parser.rs:1022) | surprising but harmless | usage_empty_arg | bug-compatible |
| S10.15 | A file named `.json` or `.toon` has no extension, so `.toon` holding TOON is parsed as JSON and fails (args.rs:97-99) | auto-detection surprise | auto_dotfile_json, auto_dotfile_toon | bug-compatible |
| S10.16 | `--stats` reports nothing when TOON is estimated larger than JSON (saturating difference, mod.rs:56, 67) and is silently ignored in decode mode | silent | stats_unicode_whitespace, stats_on_decode_ignored | bug-compatible |
| S10.17 | The percentage is computed as (D/J)·100 in binary64, so some exact ties land off the tie: 23/80 prints `-28.7%` (exact 28.75), 49/80 prints `-61.3%` (exact 61.25) (mod.rs:59) | double rounding | stats_f64_below_tie, stats_f64_above_tie | bug-compatible (needs software binary64) |
| S10.18 | A failed stderr write (`/dev/full`) aborts the process with SIGABRT instead of exiting 1; a closed stderr does not (S9.20, corrected in round 6) (main.rs:4; Cargo.toml:84) | abort on a full stderr | (case to add: none expressible — harness limitation; oracle run in the report) | Platform DISC candidate (OQ-A5) |
| S10.19 | `<prog>` in usage lines follows argv[0], the version line does not (clap:builder/command.rs:902, 4912) | inconsistent naming when the binary is renamed | usage_unknown_flag, usage_version | bug-compatible under the harness name `toon` (OQ-A3) |

### S10, part B (JSON text input and the two JSON writers): Known bugs and oddities — JSON input and output (S10.20–S10.39)

| S10.n | behavior | why it is a bug | reproduce with case | decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S10.20 | The two JSON writers spell U+0008 and U+000C differently: writer A `\b` and `\f`, writer B `\u0008` and `\u000c` (S5.68, S5.69; `ser.rs:2135-2149`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:158-161`). | One tool, one flag apart, two spellings of the same value; both are valid JSON, but byte comparison of outputs breaks. | decstr_control_out vs decstr_control_out_expand (lines `f`, `g`) | bug-compatible (DISC candidate C-3) |
| S10.21 | Writer A writes DEL U+007F and the C1 controls U+0080–U+009F RAW; writer B escapes them as `\u007f`, `\u0080`–`\u009f` (S5.70, S5.71; `ser.rs:2156-2158`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:158-161`). | Same as S10.20; in addition writer A's output carries raw terminal control bytes. | decstr_control_out vs decstr_control_out_expand (lines `c`, `d`) | bug-compatible (DISC candidate C-3) |
| S10.22 | Plain `--decode` prints repeated TOON keys as repeated JSON member names (`{"a": 1.0, "a": 2.0, …}`), while `--expand-paths safe` rejects them (strict) or keeps the last value (S5.72; `src/cli/json_stream.rs:140-162`). | JSON with duplicate names is interoperability-hostile (RFC 8259 §4), and the two modes disagree about the same input. | jsonout_duplicate_keys, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand, jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand_lenient | bug-compatible |
| S10.23 | The message `lone leading surrogate in hex escape` is also used for a lone TRAILING surrogate (`"\udc00"`), and for a high surrogate followed by a non-surrogate escape (`read.rs:911-914`, where the source itself remarks on it). | The wording is wrong for half of its triggers. | jsonerr_lone_low_surrogate, jsonerr_high_then_bmp | bug-compatible (message text is pinned) |
| S10.24 | A `\u` escape swallows the next four bytes blindly, so the closing quote and even a following LF can be "hex digits": `"\u12"` → `EOF while parsing a string` 1:6, but `"\u12"⏎` → `invalid escape` 2:0, a position on a line that contains nothing (`read.rs:621-635`). | The reported place is after the real mistake and can be on another line. | jsonerr_short_hex jsonerr_short_hex_newline, jsonerr_hex_multibyte | bug-compatible |
| S10.25 | A final newline changes the MESSAGE of several truncation errors (S2.44): `tru` → `EOF while parsing a value` but `tru⏎` → `expected ident at line 2 column 0`; likewise `1.`, `-`, `1e`, `"abc`, `"\ud800` (`de.rs:445-456`, `de.rs:553-557`). | Editors add a final newline; the same mistake reads differently with and without it. | jsonerr_bad_true, jsonerr_dot_end, jsonerr_lone_minus jsonerr_ident_newline, jsonerr_dot_newline, jsonerr_minus_newline | bug-compatible |
| S10.26 | Columns are BYTE counts that include the offending byte: they can point into the middle of a multi-byte character (`{"é😀": oops}` → column 12, the 8th character), and an offending LF is reported as `line+1 column 0`, a column no editor shows (`read.rs:421-430`, `read.rs:577-581`). | Positions do not match what a user sees in an editor for non-ASCII text. | jsonerr_unicode_in_error_col, jsonerr_raw_newline_in_string, jsonerr_whitespace | bug-compatible |
| S10.27 | `Infinity` and `NaN` are `expected value … column 1`, but `-Infinity` and `-NaN` are `invalid number … column 2` (S2.6; `de.rs:1420-1423`, `de.rs:505`). | Inconsistent diagnosis of the same kind of token. | jsonerr_infinity, jsonerr_nan, jsonerr_minus_word jsonerr_minus_infinity | bug-compatible |
| S10.28 | A UTF-8 BOM in front of valid JSON is rejected with `expected value at line 1 column 1` (S2.3; `de.rs:1455`). | Files saved by Windows tools fail with a message that does not mention the BOM. | jsonerr_bom_prefixed | bug-compatible |
| S10.29 | End of input after a comma is `EOF while parsing a value` even inside a list or an object (`[1,` and `{"a":1,`), while without the comma it is `EOF while parsing a list` / `… an object` (`de.rs:1957`, `de.rs:2013`). | Three messages for one situation (truncated container). | jsonerr_eof_in_array, jsonerr_eof_in_object jsonerr_eof_after_comma_arr, jsonerr_eof_after_comma_obj | bug-compatible |
| S10.30 | The nesting limit is tested at the opening bracket, before its content: 127 × `[` + `[]` fails although the innermost array is empty and nothing deeper exists; and the limit exists only on JSON INPUT: oracle run, plain `--decode` of a 201-level nested TOON object prints 201-level JSON, and feeding that JSON back to `--encode` fails with `recursion limit exceeded at line 128 column 260` (`de.rs:1372-1379`). | Asymmetric round trip for deep data. | jsonerr_deep_128, enc_deep_arrays_100 enc_deep_arrays_127, jsonout_deep_200 | bug-compatible |
| S10.31 | `number out of range` from an oversized exponent is reported in the MIDDLE of the token, just after the digit that crossed 2147483647 (`1e99999999999999999999` → column 12 of a 22-byte token), while overflow by value is reported at the token's end (`de.rs:606-609`, `de.rs:867`). | Two position conventions for one message. | encnum_overflow_huge_exponent, encnum_overflow_positive | bug-compatible |
| S10.32 | `--indent N` on decode is BOTH the JSON output indent and the TOON input indentation unit (S5.53), so compact JSON (`--indent 0`) is only obtainable from documents without any indented line, and the intended "same document, four indents" goldens are errors for 0, 1 and 4 (`src/cli/conversion.rs:40-56`). | A formatting flag changes which inputs are accepted; nested data cannot be printed compactly from the CLI. | jsonout_indent_0, jsonout_indent_1, jsonout_indent_4, jsonout_expand_indent_0, jsonout_expand_indent_1, jsonout_expand_indent_4, jsonout_root_array_compact | bug-compatible |
| S10.33 | JSON `-0`, `-0.0` lose their sign on input (S2.52): every zero becomes `+0` (`src/lib.rs:82-84`). Numeric detail and output text: X-C. | A valid JSON value is silently changed. | encnum_root_negative_zero | bug-compatible |
| S10.34 | The JSON error line has a doubled prefix: `JSON error: Failed to parse JSON: …` (`src/error.rs:33-34`, `src/error.rs:218-220`). | Two layers say the same thing. | jsonerr_empty | bug-compatible (text is pinned) |
| S10.35 | Writer A's and the tree builder's malformed-stream messages (S9.77–S9.82) are dead code for the CLI, and the `null` fallback for non-finite numbers in both writers is dead as well (S5.54) (`src/cli/json_stream.rs:57-61`, `src/cli/json_stringify.rs:128-132`). | Not a bug: recorded so the port does not invent cases for them. | decnum_extremes | bug-compatible (no port obligation beyond the library twins) |

### S10, part C (numbers: four text/value algorithms and their glue): Known bugs and oddities — S10.40–S10.59 (status: Amendment A3)

| S10.n | behavior | why it is a bug | reproduce with case | decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S10.40 | JSON number input is not correctly rounded: digits beyond a 64-bit significand are dropped and the result is rounded two or more times (`serde_json-1.0.151/src/de.rs:639`). | A JSON reader is expected to give the nearest binary64; the TOON printed for `0.3333333333333333333333` is `0.33333333333333337`. | encnum_long_mantissa, encnum_frac_long, encnum_pow10_slow_path, encnum_pow10_each | bug-compatible (default); DISC candidate C-2 |
| S10.41 | `1.7976931348623158e308` is rejected with `number out of range` although its correctly rounded value is the largest finite binary64 (the exact product with the rounded table entry P(292), which lies above 10^292, crosses 2^1024 − 2^970; `de.rs:651`). The decoder accepts the same token as a number. Spellings of the SAME value differ too: `17976931348623157e292` is accepted while `1797693134862315700e290` and the 309-digit integer `17976931348623157000…0` (the encoder's own TOON text for that value) are rejected. | Valid in-range input is refused; the encoder's output for the largest value is not acceptable as its input. | encnum_mixed_digits encnum_max_finite_edge_err | bug-compatible (default), encnum_max_finite_edge_ok |
| S10.42 | encnum_mixed_digits stops at its 17th literal, so its first 16 literals (among them five that differ from correct rounding: `7e23`, `3e25`, `7e-25`, `0.1e-22`, `0.7e24`) are not observed by any golden (`src/cli/conversion.rs:22`). | A corpus gap, not an original bug: the case was written to observe those values. | encnum_mixed_digits encnum_mixed_digits_ok | add the case; keep the existing one as the error golden |
| S10.43 | Decoded integers print as floats: `id: 1` → `"id": 1.0`; large ones in exponent notation (`18446744073709551615` → `1.8446744073709552e+19`) (`src/cli/json_stream.rs:214`). The original's own document says a decoded root number prints as `42` (`EXISTING_TOON_RUST_STRUCTURE.md:713`); the golden shows `42.0`-style text. | README examples show integers; the golden wins. | decnum_ints, happy_readme_users_decode, decnum_root | bug-compatible (default); DISC candidate C-1, OQ-007 |
| S10.44 | The two printers break exact ties differently: TOON rounds the last digit up, JSON picks the even digit (`src/encode/primitives.rs:92` vs `zmij-1.0.23/src/lib.rs:1495`). The same double 2101031963024178.25 is `2101031963024178.3` in TOON and `2101031963024178.2` in JSON. | Two texts for one value inside one tool; both round-trip, so it is cosmetic, but a port that shares one digit generator must parameterize the tie rule. | encnum_tie_up_1, decfmt_tie_even_1 | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.45 | TOON → JSON → TOON changes about 11% of random doubles, and JSON → TOON alone changes the digits of many 16–17 digit literals (`90775.01101792969` → `90775.01101792968`), a consequence of S10.40 (`de.rs:645`). | The tool does not round-trip its own JSON output. | encnum_text_changes | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.46 | Asymmetry at the top of the range: `1e309` is an ERROR on encode (`de.rs:664`) but a STRING on decode (`src/shared/literal_utils.rs:144`), and the decode side turns an overflowing token into a string silently. | The same token is fatal in one direction and data in the other. | encnum_overflow_positive, decnum_extremes | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.47 | The `number out of range` column for an oversized exponent points into the middle of the literal (the 10th exponent digit, column 17 of a literal ending at column 27), while the other out-of-range paths report the literal's last byte (`de.rs:604`). | Inconsistent positions for one message. | encnum_overflow_huge_exponent, encnum_overflow_positive | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.48 | A fraction digit can be accepted into S after integer digits were dropped (S4.105), so the collected significand skips positions of the literal: `184467440737095516160.0e-26` is read as 18446744073709551610 × 10^−25 (`de.rs:728`, `de.rs:539`). | The value is built from non-adjacent digits; visible only near S = 1844674407370955161. | encnum_u64_boundary encnum_longint_then_fraction | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.49 | `is_numeric_like` accepts any string that starts with two or more digits the first of which is `0`, whatever follows: `"007abc"`, `"00x"`, `"-00abc"` are quoted although no reader would take them for numbers (`src/shared/literal_utils.rs:39`). It also has no finiteness test (`"1e309"` is quoted needlessly). | Over-quoting: costs two bytes, never loses data. | encstr_quoting encstr_numeric_like_forms | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.50 | Array lengths accept a leading `+` and leading zeros (`a[+2]`, `a[007]`), and a length above 2^64 − 1 silently turns the header into a key (`a[18446744073709551616]: 1` → key `a[18446744073709551616]`) while 2^64 − 1 is a cap error: the boundary is the platform's machine word (`src/decode/parser.rs:179`). | The TOON grammar has plain digits only; the 2^64 boundary is an implementation artifact a Nat-based port must reproduce explicitly. | toonerr_huge_length toonedge_length_forms, toonerr_length_u64_max | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.51 | The original's design document says a number is encoded like JavaScript `String(value)` (`EXISTING_TOON_RUST_STRUCTURE.md:240`), which would print `1e21` as `1e+21`; the goldens show full decimal expansion (`1000000000000000000000`), never an exponent. | Documentation disagrees with behavior; the golden wins. | encnum_exponents, encnum_extremes | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.52 | Zero with an absurd exponent is accepted (`0e99999999999999999999` → `0`) while any nonzero significand with the same exponent is fatal; and leading zeros in an exponent are unlimited (`de.rs:866`, `de.rs:606`). | Harmless leniency, listed because a natural port would reject or mis-handle both. | encnum_zero_huge_exponent, encnum_overflow_huge_exponent | bug-compatible (default) |

### S10, part D (the encoder: value to TOON lines, key folding): Known bugs and oddities of the encoder (S10.60–S10.79) (status: Amendment A3)

| S10.n | behavior | why it is a bug | reproduce with case | decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S10.60 | Control characters other than U+0009, U+000A, U+000D are never escaped and do not trigger quoting (unless at an edge and White_Space: U+000B, U+000C, U+0085). `{"nul":"x\u0000y"}` → `nul: x<U+0000>y`; `{"b":"x\by"}` → `b: x<U+0008>y`; `{"del":"x\u007fy"}` → `del: x<U+007F>y`; inside quotes too: `esc: "<U+001B>[0m"`. (`src/shared/validation.rs:72-74`, `src/shared/string_utils.rs:28`) | raw NUL/ESC bytes in a text format; DISC-CANDIDATE C-5 | `goldens/encstr_escapes_in` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.61 | In a list-item object the sibling-collision check for the remaining fields does not see the item's FIRST field, so a fold can produce a key that duplicates the first field's literal key: R2 `[--key-folding safe]` `[{"c.d":7,"c":{"d":1}}]` → `[1]:⏎  - c.d: 7⏎    c.d: 1` (two `c.d` keys in one object). With the dotted key in a later position the check works: `[{"x":1,"c":{"d":1},"c.d":7}]` → `    c:⏎      d: 1⏎    c.d: 7`. (`src/encode/encoders.rs:369-373`, `src/encode/encoders.rs:449`) | the "safe" guarantee (no folded key equal to a literal sibling) is broken; the output has a duplicate key | enc_fold_list_item_dup_key | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.62 | The root-literal set holds ROOT keys only. A dotted literal key of a NESTED object protects only its own body's chain (sibling check), not the nested parts of that chain: R2 `{"r":{"s":{"p":{"q":1}},"s.p.q":9,"z":1}}` → `r:⏎  s:⏎    p.q: 1⏎  s.p.q: 9⏎  z: 1`, whereas the same shape at the root (`{"x":{"a":{"b":1},"c":2},"x.a.b":3}`) leaves `a:⏎    b: 1` unfolded. (`src/encode/encoders.rs:49-55`) | inconsistent protection: after path expansion `r.s.p.q` is spelled by two different lines | enc_fold_nested_literal_not_root | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.63 | Inside list items the flatten-depth budget restarts at the option's value and the root-literal set is absent (S4.73): R7 `[--key-folding safe --flatten-depth 2]` `{"a":{"b":[{"x":1,"c":{"d":{"e":1}}}]}}` → `a.b[1]:⏎  - x: 1⏎    c.d:⏎      e: 1`. (`src/encode/encoders.rs:389`, `src/encode/encoders.rs:443`, `src/encode/encoders.rs:449`) | the budget is per object-chain, not per document path; arrays silently reset it | enc_fold_budget_reset_in_list | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.64 | An array of uniform objects that is ITSELF a list item (an array directly inside an array) is never tabular: R14 `{"h":[[{"id":1},{"id":2}]]}` → `h[1]:⏎  - [2]:⏎    - id: 1⏎    - id: 2`, while the same inner array under a key is `[2]{id}:⏎  1⏎  2`. (`src/encode/encoders.rs:464-474`) | the tabular test is skipped in one position only; costs tokens, still decodable | `goldens/fx_enc_arrays_nested_08` shows the position with non-uniform objects enc_aoa_corners | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.65 | The leading-zero shortcut (S4.11) quotes strings that are not numbers at all: R9 `007abc` → `"007abc"`, `00:` → `"00:"`, `-01x` → `"-01x"`, `00e` → `"00e"`, while `0x10`, `12a`, `1e5x` stay bare. (`src/shared/literal_utils.rs:38-41`) | over-quoting; harmless but irregular | encstr_numeric_like | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.66 | The identifier rule looks only at the segments collected WITHIN the budget, and a failure refuses the whole fold. A smaller budget can therefore ENABLE a fold that the unlimited budget refuses: R6 `{"a":{"b":{"c-d":1}}}` → unlimited `a:⏎  b:⏎    "c-d": 1`; `[--flatten-depth 2]` `a.b:⏎  "c-d": 1`. (`src/encode/folding.rs:42-50`) | folding is not monotone in the budget | enc_fold_depth2_enables_fold | bug-compatible (default), enc_fold_unlimited_refuses |
| S10.67 | Vendored fixture `expected` vs golden: for all 147 encode fixtures the golden stdout equals the upstream `expected` text plus ONE final `\n` (R0); no other difference exists. Two fixtures make the difference visible: `objects.json#3` "encodes empty objects as empty string": expected `` (empty), golden `\n`; `whitespace.json#1` "produces no trailing newline at end of output": expected `id: 123`, golden `id: 123\n`. (`src/cli/mod.rs:187-188`) | the fixture text describes the library string; the CLI always terminates the document with a newline. The golden wins | `goldens/fx_enc_objects_03`, `goldens/fx_enc_whitespace_01` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.68 | Indentation is purely `indent × depth`. Remaining fields of a list-item object sit at depth i+1, which lines up under the first field's key only when indent is 2 (`- ` is two characters): indent 1 → ` - x: 1⏎  y[2]:`; indent 3 → `   - p: 1⏎      z: 0`. With indent 0 all nesting disappears: `{"a":{"b":1},"l":[{"x":1},{"y":2}]}` → `a:⏎b: 1⏎l[2]:⏎- x: 1⏎- y: 2`, which no longer denotes the input. (`src/encode/encoders.rs:486-496`, `src/encode/encoders.rs:449`) | indent 0 output is lossy; other indents break visual alignment | `goldens/flag_indent_0_encode`, `goldens/flag_indent_1_encode` | bug-compatible (default), enc_list_first_field_shapes_pipe_indent3 |
| S10.69 | Unicode line-breaking characters are data, never structure: U+2028, U+2029, U+0085, U+000B, U+000C in the MIDDLE of a string leave it bare and raw (`ls: a<U+2028>b`); at an EDGE they force quotes but stay raw inside them (`nel: "<U+0085>x"`, R13 `vt: "<U+000B>"`). (`src/shared/validation.rs:52-54`, `src/shared/validation.rs:72-74`) | a consumer that splits lines on Unicode line terminators sees a broken document | `goldens/encstr_unicode` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.70 | README.md:105-107 shows key folding of a two-field object as two fully folded lines (`config.database.host: localhost⏎config.database.port: 5432`). The original folds only single-field chains: `config.database:⏎  host: localhost⏎  port: 5432`. (`src/encode/folding.rs:97-99`) | documentation disagrees with behavior; the golden wins | `goldens/happy_config_folded` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.71 | README.md:368 says tabular detection requires "identical keys in the same order". The original requires the same key SET; order may differ and cells follow the first row's order (S4.36, S4.37). (`src/encode/encoders.rs:320-340`) | documentation disagrees with behavior; the golden wins | `goldens/fx_enc_arrays_objects_15`, `goldens/enc_shapes_mixed` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.72 | README.md:402 says folding is prevented when it "would exceed `--flatten-depth`". The original folds UP TO the budget and nests the rest (S4.77). (`src/encode/folding.rs:92`) | documentation disagrees with behavior; the golden wins | `goldens/fx_enc_key_folding_06`, `goldens/flag_flatten_depth_3` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.73 | The original's own structure document (EXISTING_TOON_RUST_STRUCTURE.md:299-302) says an array of primitive arrays writes each row as `- a,b,c`. The original writes `- [N]: a,b,c` (S4.33). (`src/encode/encoders.rs:235-236`) | documentation disagrees with behavior; the golden wins | `goldens/fx_enc_arrays_nested_01` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.74 | A literal dotted key is written bare (`a.b: 2`) right next to folded keys (`a.b.c: 1`), with folding on or off; the output does not record which dots are literal, so a decoder with path expansion cannot recover the literal key. (`src/shared/validation.rs:16`, `src/encode/encoders.rs:149`) | information loss for `--expand-paths safe` round trips (same as the upstream reference) | `goldens/enc_folding_mix`, `goldens/encstr_keys` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.75 | Asymmetric rules: the key rule is ASCII-only and ignores literals (`"é": 8` quoted, `true: 13` bare) while the value rule is Unicode-permissive and literal-aware (`cafe: café` bare, `t: "true"` quoted). (`src/shared/validation.rs:5-23`, `src/shared/validation.rs:47-85`) | surprising but intended by the format; listed so nobody "harmonizes" the two rules | `goldens/encstr_keys`, `goldens/encstr_unicode`, `goldens/encstr_quoting` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.76 | The absolute path of the root-literal check is a raw dot-join (S4.71): a nested key that itself contains dots contributes several apparent segments. R15 `{"u":{"v.w":{"a":{"b":1}}},"u.v.w.a.b":1}` → `u:⏎  v.w:⏎    a:⏎      b: 1⏎u.v.w.a.b: 1`: the unrelated root key `u.v.w.a.b` blocks the fold of `a.b` under `u` → `v.w`. (`src/encode/encoders.rs:89-90`, `src/encode/folding.rs:65-68`) | path ambiguity; conservative (refuses a fold), never produces a wrong document | enc_fold_dotted_parent_path | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.77 | The rules "non-finite number → null" (normalization and number text) are dead through the CLI: no JSON text yields a non-finite value (S3.5). (`src/encode/normalize.rs:22-23`, `src/encode/primitives.rs:89-91`) | unreachable behavior; the port needs no case and no code path for it unless a library driver is added | `goldens/encnum_overflow_positive` (shows the reader's rejection instead) | bug-compatible (default) |

### S10, part E (the decoder: TOON lines to events and values, path expansion): Known bugs and oddities — decode side (S10.80–S10.99) (status: Amendment A3)

**Vendored fixtures versus the pinned binary.** All 202 `fx_dec_*` fixtures were compared mechanically with the
upstream expectations in `cases/fixtures/spec/decode/*.json`: the 23 fixtures that upstream marks as error cases
all exit 1 in the goldens, and the 179 others all exit 0 with a JSON value equal to upstream's `expected`
(same keys, same key order, same values when numbers are compared numerically). **No fixture is accepted by one
side and rejected by the other.** Two differences remain and are recorded below: the reason of one rejection
(S10.86) and the text of integer-valued numbers (S10.96). The goldens win in both.

| S10.n | behavior | why it is a bug | reproduce with case | decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S10.80 | In a root object, the first line with depth ≠ 0 that no block consumed ends the decode; that line and ALL following lines, including later depth-0 keys, vanish without message, exit 0 (`src/decode/decoders.rs:81`). Typical triggers: a line indented deeper than its siblings; rows / items left over after a lenient array; an indented line after a primitive-valued key. Probe: `a:⏎␠␠b: 1⏎␠␠␠␠c: 2⏎␠␠d: 3⏎e: 4⏎` → `{"a":{"b":1.0}}`. | silent data loss: three keys of the document never reach the output and nothing is reported | `goldens/toonlenient_extra_list_items`, `goldens/toonlenient_extra_tabular_rows` (the surplus line is dropped instead of parsed) toonedge_rest_dropped_after_overindent, toonlenient_leftover_rows_drop_rest | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.81 | After a root array is complete every remaining line is ignored, exit 0 (`src/decode/decoders.rs:60`). Probe: `[2]: a,b⏎c: 1⏎` → `["a","b"]`; `[1]:⏎␠␠- a⏎zzz` → `["a"]`. | trailing content after a root array is a malformed document, accepted silently | toonedge_root_array_trailing_ignored | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.82 | Over-indented children are accepted in strict mode: the first child fixes the sibling depth (`src/decode/decoders.rs:157`), so `a:⏎␠␠␠␠b: 1` (a jump of two levels) decodes as `{"a":{"b":1.0}}`. | strict mode checks multiples of the indent size but never that depth grows by one | `goldens/toonerr_depth_jump` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.83 | **RESOLVED by the re-pin to `7c1d6e4` (2026-09-22): a `[` after the key colon is value text (S2.130), so every example below now decodes to its string and round-trips; the owner ordered the fix, C-10.** Before: On a key-value line that does not start with `"`, the header parser takes the first `[` of the whole line, even inside a quoted VALUE (`src/decode/parser.rs:53`). A value such as `"x[1]: y"` is therefore read as an array header: `a: "x[1]: y"` → `{"a: \"x":["y\""]}`. The original's own encoder emits exactly this line for the JSON `{"a":"x[1]: y"}`, so encode → decode does not round-trip such strings. Only values containing `[`, optional `+`, digits, optional TAB or `\|`, `]` and a later `:` are hit; `"[abc]: y"` and `"x[1] y"` survive. List-item strings, tabular cells, inline values and root primitives are not affected. EXACT REACH (pass 2): only the FIRST `[` of the line is tried (S2.130), it needs a length inside (S2.135) and the fields-aware header colon of S2.132 after it, so `"[abc] [1]: y"`, `"x[1] y"`, `"x[1\t]: y"` (backslash-`t` written by the encoder) and `"x[1]{p:q}"` survive. OTHER OUTCOMES: an error instead of silent corruption (`a: "[1]:"` → S9.103; `a: "x[2]: y"` → S9.108; the cap, S10.84). POSITION: the first field of a list-item OBJECT is a key-value line and is hit too (`- e: "x[1]: y"`). | round-trip corruption of legitimate string values; key and value both wrong, exit 0 | toonedge_header_in_quoted_value, enc_value_looks_like_header, toonedge_header_in_value_first_bracket_only, toonerr_header_in_value_unterminated, toonerr_header_in_value_count, toonedge_header_in_value_list_item_field | bug-compatible (default); candidate for the owner's DISC list |
| S10.84 | **RESOLVED by the re-pin to `7c1d6e4` (2026-09-22): the example below decodes to `{"note":"see [999999999999]: x"}`; the cap still fires on a real header (S2.136).** Before: The length cap fires for any line that reaches S2.136, also for a bracket inside a quoted value or prose: `note: "see [999999999999]: x"` → `ERR: Declared array length 999999999999 exceeds maximum allowed (100000000)` (`src/decode/parser.rs:108`). | a string value makes the whole document undecodable | toonerr_cap_in_quoted_value | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.85 | The strict surplus-item check looks only for a line starting with `- ` (`src/decode/validation.rs:40`), while the item reader also accepts a bare `-`. A surplus bare `-` item is not reported: `l[1]:⏎␠␠- a⏎␠␠-⏎` → `{"l":["a"]}`, exit 0 (and by S10.80 the rest of the document is dropped). | strict mode accepts a list longer than its declared length | toonerr_extra_bare_dash_undetected | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.86 | Fixture `blank-lines.json#5` (`outer[2]:⏎␠␠- inner[2]:⏎␠␠␠␠- a⏎⏎␠␠␠␠- b⏎␠␠- x`): upstream expects a blank-line error; the pinned binary rejects it with `Expected 2 list array items, but got 0`, because a list that is the first field of a list item must have its items at item depth + 2 (S4.224, `src/decode/decoders.rs:450`) and the fixture places them at + 1. Without the blank line the binary still rejects the document with the same message (probe). Both sides exit with an error; only the reason differs. | the message names the wrong defect for this input | `goldens/fx_dec_blank_lines_05` | bug-compatible (default): the golden message is the contract |
| S10.87 | Inline values win over a fields segment (`src/decode/decoders.rs:186`): `t[2]{a,b}: 1,2` → `{"t":[1.0,2.0]}`; the field names are parsed (their errors surface) and then ignored. `[2]{a}: 1` → the inline count message. | a tabular header with inline values is malformed; it is accepted as a primitive array | toonedge_inline_beats_fields | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.88 | Text between `]` and `{`, `}` and `:`, `]` and `:` is ignored (S2.133, `src/decode/parser.rs:81`): `items[2][3]: a,b`, `"a"[1]extra: x`, `items[2]extra{a,b}:` are accepted. | malformed headers are accepted silently | `goldens/toonerr_header_spaces` (the spaces variant) toonedge_header_ignored_text | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.89 | **RESOLVED by the re-pin to `7c1d6e4` (2026-09-22): a key cannot contain a colon (S2.130, S2.134); `a: [1]: x` → `{"a":"[1]: x"}`, `note: see [1] for details: ok` → `{"note":"see [1] for details: ok"}`.** Before: The header key is everything before the first `[`, so it can contain a colon: `a: [1]: x` → `{"a:":["x"]}`; `a: [2]: x` → the inline count message; `note: see [1] for details: ok` → `{"note: see":["ok"]}` (`src/decode/parser.rs:89`). | a key-value line whose value looks like a header is re-read as a keyed array | `goldens/toonerr_value_is_header_like` toonedge_header_key_with_colon | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.90 | The key-token reader decides "quoted" from the first BYTE of the content (`src/decode/parser.rs:346`), while other tests trim first. A quoted key preceded by whitespace keeps its quote characters and counts as unquoted: lenient `⇥"a": 1` → `{"\"a\"":1.0}`; strict `l[1]:⏎␠␠-␠␠"a": b` (two spaces after the hyphen) → `{"l":[{"\"a\"":"b"}]}`; `-␠␠"a:b": 1` → `[{"\"a":"b\": 1"}]`. | the quotes become part of the key and a colon inside the quotes splits the key | toonlenient_tab_quoted_key, toonedge_list_item_two_spaces_quoted_key | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.91 | Quoted-ness is remembered per key STRING per object (S3.26, `src/decode/event_builder.rs:165`): a quoted `"a.b"` stops the expansion of an unquoted `a.b` in the same object, even one that comes earlier. | an unquoted dotted key is not expanded because of an unrelated entry | toonedge_expand_quoted_unquoted_same_key, toonedge_expand_quoted_unquoted_same_key_lenient | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.92 | CRLF input: a bare `-` item arrives as `-␍` and is not recognised (S2.113, `src/decode/decoders.rs:322`), while every other construct tolerates the CR. | a CRLF copy of a valid document fails with a count message | `goldens/toonerr_crlf` (the tolerated constructs) toonedge_crlf_bare_dash | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.93 | A UTF-8 BOM is kept (S2.114, `src/cli/conversion.rs:45`): it becomes the first character of the first key, and a root array header behind a BOM becomes an object with the key U+FEFF. | BOM-prefixed files decode to a different value | `goldens/toonerr_bom` toonedge_bom_root_array | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.94 | The strict tab check looks at the whole leading space/tab run (`src/decode/scanner.rs:75`), so a tab-delimited row whose first cell is empty and unquoted (`␠␠⇥2`) fails with the tab message; lenient mode decodes it as `{"a":"","b":2.0}`. | a valid tab-delimited row is rejected as bad indentation | toonerr_tab_delim_empty_first_cell | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.95 | Duplicate keys: without expansion both are written (the output is a JSON text with a repeated name); with `--expand-paths safe` the same document is a strict conflict or, leniently, last-write-wins (S4.247, `src/decode/event_builder.rs:186`). | the flag changes the meaning of documents that contain no dotted key at all | `goldens/jsonout_duplicate_keys`, `goldens/jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand`, `goldens/jsonout_duplicate_keys_expand_lenient` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.96 | Upstream's `expected` values hold integers (`{"id":1}`); the goldens print every decoded number through the float writer (`1.0`). 79 of the 179 accepted fixtures contain an integer-valued number and differ textually this way (for example `fx_dec_arrays_primitive_02`, `fx_dec_objects_01`, `fx_dec_primitives_13`, `fx_dec_numbers_13`); the values are numerically equal. Owned by the number part (DISC candidate C-1, OQ-007); recorded here because it is the only fixture-wide difference (`src/decode/parser.rs:265`). | README examples show integers | `goldens/fx_dec_primitives_13`, `goldens/fx_dec_arrays_primitive_02` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.97 | Declared lengths accept Rust's unsigned syntax (S2.135, `src/decode/parser.rs:179`): `a[+2]: x,y` and `b[02]: x,y` are arrays of two, while `c[ 2]: x,y` is the key `c[ 2]`. A length of 18446744073709551616 (2^64) is "not a header" while 18446744073709551615 is a cap error. | `+` and leading zeros are not TOON; the 2^64 boundary is a platform artefact (64-bit `usize`) | toonedge_length_plus_and_zero, toonedge_length_u64_overflow, toonerr_length_u64_max | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.98 | "Is this a key-value line" has two definitions: at the root (S4.203) any `:` byte counts, quotes ignored; in a list item (S4.224) only a `:` outside quotes counts. `a"b: c` is the object `{"a\"b":"c"}` at the root and the string `a"b: c` as a list item (`src/decode/decoders.rs:503`, `src/decode/parser.rs:362`). | the same text means different things in the two positions | toonedge_root_vs_item_keyvalue | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.99 | Field-segment corner cases (S2.137, `src/decode/parser.rs:122`): `items[1]{}:` is a list array; `items[1]{ }:` is a table with one field named by the empty string (`items[1]{ }:⏎␠␠- 1` → `[{"":"- 1"}]`); `{,}` gives two empty names; `{a,a}` gives a row object with the key `a` twice. | malformed headers are accepted | toonedge_fields_space_only | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.200 | Second encode → decode break (the first is S10.83). A tabular field name that contains `}` is written by the encoder in quotes with the brace raw inside them (S4.21, S4.26): `[{"a}b":1,"c":2},{"a}b":3,"c":4}]` → `[2]{"a}b",c}:⏎  1,2⏎  3,4` [run]. The decoder finds the end of the fields segment with a plain byte search (S2.132), cuts it at that brace, and the cut piece `"a` fails as a string literal: `Unterminated string: missing closing quote`, exit 1, stdout empty [run]. The outcome is ALWAYS that error, never a silent wrong value [run: 40 generated names]. An opening brace in a field name is harmless (`[1]{"a{b",c}:` decodes) [run]. (`src/decode/parser.rs:75`, `src/encode/primitives.rs:70-79`) | the encoder emits a document that its own decoder rejects | enc_tabular_field_name_close_brace, toonerr_field_name_close_brace, toonedge_field_name_open_brace_ok | bug-compatible (default); candidate for the owner's DISC list |

## S11. Performance characteristics of the original

| S11.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S11.1 | The original is single-threaded and processes one document per process: it reads the whole input into memory, converts it, and writes the whole output; no output byte is produced before the conversion has succeeded (S4.1, S5.100). Work and memory are linear in the input size for both directions, except key-folding sibling checks and tabular header lookups, which are linear per field in the number of sibling keys (quadratic in the width of one object, never in the document). | `src/cli/mod.rs:29-125`, `src/encode/encoders.rs:36-75`, `src/encode/encoders.rs:481-483` | large_tabular_1500, large_tabular_1500_pipe_folded |
| S11.2 | The hot loops are per character and per row: on encode, the JSON reader's scan and the per-cell quoting decision and join of tabular rows; on decode, the line scanner, the per-row delimiter split, the token classifier and the JSON writer. Rows of one tabular array are independent of each other in both directions, which is the parallel seam the port plans for (PROPOSED_ARCHITECTURE §7). | `src/encode/encoders.rs:282-305`, `src/decode/decoders.rs:236-300`, `src/decode/parser.rs:183-222` | large_tabular_1500 |
| S11.3 | Baseline of the pinned release binary alone on this host (AMD EPYC-Milan, 8 CPUs, Linux 7.0.0-30, 2026-09-20; median of 15 sequential runs, process start included, output to /dev/null): encode of `cases/inputs/hand/large_tabular_1500.json` (167255 bytes, 1500 rows) 5.48 ms (cv 6.7%); decode of its 76989-byte TOON output 13.00 ms (cv 3.7%); `--version` 0.84 ms (cv 6.0%). Two of the three exceed the 5% cv gate, so these are maintenance numbers that size the problem, not claims; the comparison of record is `scripts/incumbent-bench.sh --pin` in Phase 5 with interleaved runs. | measured with `./oracle/toon` (`docs/PLAN_TO_PORT_Toon_TO_BEND2.md:33` pins it); the original's `README.md:139` and `README.md:149` state 3–24 ms encode and 59 ms decode for other inputs on other hardware | large_tabular_1500 |
| S11.4 | The original's README lists its own optimizations (pre-sized buffers, reference-based sibling tracking, direct string building, single-buffer JSON stringify). None of them is observable in any output; they are not behavior and are not ported as such. | `README.md:171`, `FEATURE_PARITY.md:83` | determinism_a, determinism_a_again |
